# Triage: Scout 2026-11-21 Batch

Date: 2026-11-21
Source scout: research/scouts/scout-2026-11-21.md
Reviewer: Human + Codex + agy + Claude

Limitation: The window ran from 2026-10-25 to 2026-11-21 by OAI metadata datestamp, so re-versioned older papers appear. The scout reported no category cap reached and no throttling. Two labelers judged all 893 labeled entries from full arXiv abstracts, and a third judged the 160 relevance splits from the same abstracts. The first two agreed on relevance for 733 of 893 entries (Cohen kappa 0.65). Of the 160 splits, 157 were kept by Codex only, and the tie-break kept 13 of those. These agreement figures are this repo's own labeling measurements. Under the calibrated relevance rule, findings about the check are kept and findings about the thing checked or trained are dropped. The material read was limited to arXiv abstracts. Promote decisions recommend source review. Approval of findings and canonical changes remains with the maintainer.

Selection: Full records cover all three challenges and prioritize measured limits of checks, checkable mechanisms, and techniques that can be applied to agent work. Remaining keeps retain their supplied labels in Deferred. Findings are author-reported abstract claims pending source review. Confidence describes how directly the abstract supports each record; every full record is rated medium because abstract-only evidence is treated as a common ceiling, whether the abstract reports a number directly or the record proposes a transfer. Initial labels are the intake labels from the labeling pass. Where independent review of this note (Lookout, 2026-11-21) read a result more narrowly than its intake label, the record keeps the label and states the narrower reading under What Would Change In The Doc. One intake keep, arXiv:2610.32423, is listed under Ignore by editorial disposition after that review.

## Candidate: Who Convinced Whom? Tracing How LLMs Change Their Minds in Multi-Agent Systems

Source: [arXiv:2610.35930](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35930)
Initial label: challenges
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-10-18
- Updated: 2026-10-28
- OAI datestamp: 2026-10-28
- Categories: cs.MA, cs.AI
- Authors: Arnesh Batra, Aniket Khandelwal, Jashn Khemani, Krish Thukral, Arush Gumber, Denis Federico Lim, +2 more

### Abstract Paraphrase

The study compares peer debate with round-matched independent reflection across three open-weight models.

### Key Findings

- On fully parsed non-numeric questions with reference labels, debate improved accuracy slightly over initial voting, yet produced 3.1 to 7.4 percentage points more unanimous errors than reflection.

### Why It Might Matter

A panel can agree more readily after its members see one another's answers while becoming more likely to share a mistake.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 2 and 8 could call for a round-matched independent baseline and separate reporting of unanimous errors. The challenge is to treating debate consensus as corroboration. The study uses peer discussion, so it can narrow Principle 8 without directly testing the two-sided, judged format named there.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: POIROT: Agents as their own auditors for failure attribution in large language model multi-agent systems

Source: [arXiv:2606.02282](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02282)
Initial label: challenges
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-12
- Updated: 2026-11-13
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-13
- Categories: cs.AI
- Authors: Iñaki Dellibarda Varela, R. Sendra-Arranz, Pablo Romero-Sorozabal, J. M. Valverde-García, Annemarie F. Laudanski, Álvaro Gutiérrez, +2 more

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors test whether agents can locate the source of a multi-agent failure by voting privately, sharing assessments, or questioning one another.

### Key Findings

- Private assessment beat a single-model evaluator in all nine tested model and benchmark combinations, by up to 50 percentage points. Sharing assessments hurt attribution, and interrogation failed to restore the loss.

### Why It Might Matter

Agents may have useful local evidence even when discussion makes their attributions converge on the wrong component.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 2 and 8 could distinguish privately pooling local diagnoses from asking participants to validate their own work, and compare discussion with a private-vote baseline. Review reading: the intake label is challenges, but the result is narrower. It bears on where an external evaluator is needed and supports independent assessment. It does not test whether conditioning on another agent's draft is safe, or whether adversarial debate with a judge works.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Conduct Under Pressure: What Sixty Language Models Do When a User Pushes

Source: [arXiv:2609.25447](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25447)
Initial label: challenges
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-03
- Updated: 2026-11-04
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-04
- Categories: cs.CL, cs.HC
- Authors: Tapan Parikh

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors examine how 60 models respond to fixed multi-turn pressure scenarios. A second set of scenarios was preregistered after the coding scheme was developed.

### Key Findings

- Folding was negatively associated with the capability index in both scenario sets. Model coders were more consistent than human coders on the reported comparison, which included 50 held-out transcripts labeled by humans.

### Why It Might Matter

Pressure tests can measure whether a model changes position across turns. The human-coded subset limits conclusions about replacing human review.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 4 and 5 could distinguish whether a model yielded from how it responded and use an adjudicated sample to test the coding scheme. Review reading: the intake label is challenges, but the study varies model capability and user pressure. It does not isolate model size and does not vary how a verification prompt is framed. The part that bears on checking is the independent coding and its comparison with human labels, a narrower Principle 4 and 5 contribution.

### Decision

Decision: keep-in-triage

## Candidate: What Do LLM Judges Actually Verify? Matched Controls and Record Gaps for Document Currency

Source: [arXiv:2611.01311](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01311)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-10-31
- Updated: 2026-11-03
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-03
- Categories: cs.AI, cs.CL
- Authors: Unnati Shah, Samruddhi Kakade, Aum Patel

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors vary the completeness of document amendment records while holding citation cases fixed, with controls for valid earlier citations and updates that leave a document in force.

### Key Findings

- Across 24 deliberately omitted replacement links, each of four judges missed more faults with the incomplete record than with the texts alone. Warnings recovered some detections and brought back false alarms on updates.

### Why It Might Matter

An apparently authoritative record can suppress scrutiny when its coverage is unknown.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 4 and 5 could use matched missing-link controls and separately report missed replacements and false flags on valid updates.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Swapping the Judge Changes the Ranking: A Preregistered Study of Cross-Provider Replication in LLM-Judged System Comparisons

Source: [arXiv:2611.04090](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04090)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-03
- Updated: 2026-11-04
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-04
- Categories: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.CY
- Authors: Sangdo Han, Moonsoo Kim

### Abstract Paraphrase

Five judges from three providers re-score 4,000 human-voted comparisons in both response orders.

### Key Findings

- Agreement with human votes had median kappa 0.22. Rank agreement with humans varied from Kendall tau 0.17 to 0.89. Judge disagreements with human votes co-occurred more than independence predicts, although only one human vote was available per battle.

### Why It Might Matter

Provider diversity can improve a screening rule while leaving shared errors and a substantial number of unresolved comparisons.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 2 and 7 could require reporting human alignment, joint errors and coverage when judge replication is used to substantiate a ranking.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Do Verifier Judgments Survive Abstraction? Measuring Verification Transport for LLM Agent Evaluation

Source: [arXiv:2611.18471](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18471)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-13
- Updated: 2026-11-16
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-16
- Categories: cs.CL, cs.AI
- Authors: Disha Sheshanarayana, Sai Linisha Reddy Goli

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors show verifiers native, abstracted and restored views of the same frozen runs, holding the evaluation target and ground truth fixed.

### Key Findings

- Judgment transport failures reached about 31% under macro abstraction and delegation. Restoring provenance recovered 85.5% of failures overall. Direct and deliberative protocols differed in their tendency to issue wrong verdicts or abstain.

### Why It Might Matter

An evaluation calibrated on complete traces can behave differently on the summaries or tool views available in deployment.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 3 and 5 could add matched-view checks at delegation and compaction boundaries, with abstentions tracked separately from errors.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: When Evaluators of AI Agents Disagree: An Empirical Study of Native Scoring, LLM Judges, and Commit-Boundary Checks on tau2-bench

Source: [arXiv:2611.14581](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14581)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-10
- Updated: 2026-11-13
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-13
- Categories: cs.SE, cs.AI, cs.CR
- Authors: Sungbin Im

### Abstract Paraphrase

The study compares final-state scoring, transcript judges and a database-write check on 2,160 airline and retail benchmark episodes.

### Key Findings

- A judge repeated 98.2% of its verdicts but agreed with native scoring 56.2% of the time. The write check caught all 11 observed forbidden writes, while the stronger judge still passed six.

### Why It Might Matter

Agreement across repeats measures stability. Observing writes directly tests whether an expected authorization boundary held.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 3 and 6 could call for recording actual effects at commit time and calculating agreement separately for task success and authorization.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Done Is Not Correct: Measuring Silent Failures and Self-Verification Calibration When LLM Agents Take CAD Actions

Source: [arXiv:2611.10063](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10063)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-06
- Updated: 2026-11-10
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-10
- Categories: cs.SE, cs.LG
- Authors: Carson Rodrigues, Clive Rodrigues, Aravind Reddy G

### Abstract Paraphrase

The benchmark checks the geometry produced by CAD agents against machine-testable requirements and compares completion reporting across architectures.

### Key Findings

- A measure-before-completion loop reduced reported silent failures from 14.1% to 3.2%. Tightening the oracle turned 18 previously passing verdicts into failures, and an expert audit overturned three silent-failure judgments outside the ambiguous task.

### Why It Might Matter

Completion claims can be evaluated against semantic properties of the produced object. The checker itself needs validation against the task specification.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 5 and 6 could connect completion claims to measured requirements and report sensitivity to oracle changes and disputed criteria.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Mutation Testing for Multi-Agent Workflows

Source: [arXiv:2611.09895](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09895)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-06-25
- Updated: 2026-11-10
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-10
- Categories: cs.SE
- Authors: Nafiseh Kahani, Mojtaba Bagherzadeh

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors test whether multi-agent workflow suites detect deliberate changes to coordination, restrictions and tool behavior during execution.

### Key Findings

- On ten workflows, the strongest development baseline detected 71.7% of mutations under the structural criterion and 55.9% under the safety criterion. Restriction removals were among the least detected changes.

### Why It Might Matter

A suite that passes ordinary workflows can still miss violations of the tool and delegation rules it is expected to enforce.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principle 6 could include mutation sensitivity as a way to audit check coverage. This supports stress-testing permission coverage in the FAVA seed, research/reviewed/2026-08-19-fava-permission-graphs.md.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Truthgate: Checking Coding Agents Against a Record They Cannot Edit - Cheating, Deterrence and Disclosure on Impossible Tasks

Source: [arXiv:2611.23335](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23335)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-17
- Updated: 2026-11-19
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-19
- Categories: cs.CE, cs.SE
- Authors: Bravish Ghosh

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors compare coding-agent completion claims with host-run tests and execution records held outside the agent's writable state.

### Key Findings

- On the impossible-task study, one model obtained passing scores on 39 of 180 tasks despite the specification being unsatisfied. The model judge and deterministic checks detected different evidence, and the abstract distinguishes a scripted record-integrity demonstration from observed agent behavior.

### Why It Might Matter

A model's report can be evaluated against evidence the model did not control. Deterministic checks and interpretation of that evidence can cover different failure modes.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 1 and 9 could distinguish independent custody of execution evidence from the methods used to interpret it.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: CAREFUL-Bench: Effect-Aware, Judge-Free Evaluation of Multi-Turn LLM Function Calling

Source: [arXiv:2611.02978](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02978)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-03
- Updated: 2026-11-04
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-04
- Categories: cs.AI, cs.SE
- Authors: Alexander Kovalenko, Artem Redchych

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors replay tool-use logs against a simulator to add observed effects, confirmation status and surplus work to final-state scores.

### Key Findings

- The scorer reproduced benchmark results on 800 reference trajectories. Among 11 open models, rankings were almost unchanged, yet the leading model lost 14 points for unrequested destructive calls under effect-aware scoring.

### Why It Might Matter

Similar rankings can conceal consequential differences in the paths agents took to reach the final state.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 3 and 6 could score effects and confirmations separately from final-state success. The study supports checking request-to-effect bounds in research/reviewed/2026-08-19-intent-governed-tool-authorization.md.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Does the Judge See What the Evaluation Requires? Input Assurance for LLM Agent Evaluation

Source: [arXiv:2611.02676](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02676)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-02
- Updated: 2026-11-03
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-03
- Categories: cs.SE
- Authors: Boming Xia, Qinghua Lu, Runze Li, Dino Sejdinovic, Liming Zhu

### Abstract Paraphrase

The study audits the pipeline that turns agent runs into packets for an LLM judge, including content coverage, failed-check handling and the identity of consumed evidence.

### Key Findings

- In one 30-trajectory pipeline, 53 of 60 judged steps lacked the structured call even though hashes matched. Added controls stopped 18 of 19 constructed faults before the judge stub consumed a packet.

### Why It Might Matter

Integrity checks can establish which bytes reached a judge while missing whether those bytes contain the evidence it needs.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 5, 6 and 9 could specify content coverage and verify that the last checked evidence is the evidence the evaluator actually consumes.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Measuring Collapse and Correction in Homogeneous-Panel LLM Debate

Source: [arXiv:2609.35279](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35279)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-08
- Updated: 2026-11-10
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-10
- Categories: cs.CL
- Authors: Xin Li, Mengbing Liu, Chau Yuen

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors replay homogeneous debates and distinguish initially correct majorities that become wrong from initially wrong majorities that are corrected.

### Key Findings

- In 6,925 debates, the analysis found 253 collapses. The replay estimated that freezing every debate prevented 251 collapses and lost 804 corrections under equal weights.

### Why It Might Matter

A debate intervention can look favorable when its score counts only the errors it prevents.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principle 8 could record both directions of change and specify how the costs of collapse and missed correction are weighted.

### Decision

Decision: keep-in-triage

## Candidate: Optimistic Verifiers Get Hacked: Reward Hacking Under an Exact Process Oracle

Source: [arXiv:2611.21538](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21538)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-16
- Updated: 2026-11-17
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-17
- Categories: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.CL
- Authors: Arunkumar V, Manoranjan Gandhudi, Gangadharan G. R., Arun Prakash, S. Senthilkumar

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors compare several deliberately imperfect training verifiers against an executable policy oracle that scores the same rollouts.

### Key Findings

- For one proxy, its score reached 0.940 as oracle-measured faithfulness fell from 0.731 to 0.667 on 96 held-out episodes. The broad pattern recurred across tested settings, with variable effect sizes.

### Why It Might Matter

A high verifier score can hide worsening agreement with the property the verifier was meant to measure.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 4 and 6 could use an executable reference to measure how proxy error affects selection in a scoped testbed.

### Decision

Decision: keep-in-triage

## Candidate: Signed but Not Safe: Requirement Integrity in Multi-Agent LLM Handoffs

Source: [arXiv:2611.07483](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07483)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-17
- Updated: 2026-11-18
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-18
- Categories: cs.CR, cs.AI, cs.MA
- Authors: Xiaobo Chen, Genliang Zhu, Ziyuan Wang, Chu Wang

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors compare free-text, structured and signed handoffs on synthetic tasks with separately scorable requirements.

### Key Findings

- Signing protected requirements during transfer in the tested setup. Remaining unsafe commits reflected requirements represented incorrectly before signing. A pre-signing check improved protection on fixed templates while refusing some benign requests.

### Why It Might Matter

The signed record can preserve an incorrect interpretation, making the request-to-manifest mapping a separate check.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 5 and 9 could test extraction independently from transfer integrity. The residual errors challenge relying on enforced certificates without checking their semantic inputs in research/reviewed/2026-08-19-intent-governed-tool-authorization.md.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Accepted but Wrong: Silent Failures of LLM Code Repairs after API Migrations

Source: [arXiv:2611.08269](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08269)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-13
- Updated: 2026-11-16
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-16
- Categories: cs.SE, cs.AI
- Authors: Xiaobo Chen, Genliang Zhu, Ziyuan Wang, Chu Wang

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors execute candidate API migration repairs on real runtimes and use withheld contracts to measure wrong releases.

### Key Findings

- Typical-input assertions released 41.0% of wrong repairs in the main repair space. A release-note-derived edge case reduced this to 6.5%, though harder distractors still passed at higher rates.

### Why It Might Matter

Passing tests on common inputs can obscure exactly the behavioral change a migration is meant to handle.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 4 and 6 could derive a check from the documented change and measure the residual false-pass rate on plausible wrong repairs.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Evidence-Guided Recovery for AI Agents with Ambiguous Tool Outcomes and Partial Execution

Source: [arXiv:2610.41453](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41453)
Initial label: operational technique

- Updated: 2026-11-02
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-02
- Categories: cs.AI
- Authors: Genliang Zhu, Xiaobo Chen, Ziyuan Wang, Chu Wang

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors test evidence-based recovery for ambiguous tool outcomes with a separate adjudicator that checks effect histories.

### Key Findings

- Under the same protective checks, advice based on planning certified 30 of 58 completions against nine for evidence prompting. Independent replay passed all 116 paired trajectories. Guarantees depend on the modeled provider capabilities.

### Why It Might Matter

Recovery checks can require evidence that the prior operation committed, failed or remains unresolved before a retry is deemed admissible.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 3 and 9 could track unresolved effects explicitly and scope completion certificates to provider capabilities.

## Candidate: Call-Level Revision Contracts for LLM-Authored Workflows

Source: [arXiv:2611.06967](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06967)
Initial label: operational technique
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-04
- Updated: 2026-11-06
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-06
- Categories: cs.SE
- Authors: Chenyang Jiang, Benyang Pan, Puqu Wu

### Abstract Paraphrase

A workflow revision contract validates whether a repair preserves committed calls and rechecks their payloads during replay.

### Key Findings

- In the media workload, 22 accepted repairs replayed 258 committed call identities without a duplicate. Relocating a committed call leads to a stop because callsite identity does not preserve that move.

### Why It Might Matter

Revision acceptance can include preservation of already committed work and verification of the payloads reused on replay.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 3 and 6 could add call-level replay checks for workflows with costs or side effects.

### Decision

Decision: keep-in-triage

## Candidate: Invariant-Aware Harness Enforcement to Protect LLM Agents from Silent API Failures

Source: [arXiv:2611.20595](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20595)
Initial label: narrows
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-11-16
- Updated: 2026-11-17
- OAI datestamp: 2026-11-17
- Categories: cs.CL
- Authors: Sajib Acharjee Dip, Brian Ouyang, Lala Motlhabi

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors compare harness-enforced invariants with warnings when API calls return plausible but incorrect results.

### Key Findings

- On targeted faults across seven open models, wrong answers fell from 74.0% to 1.7% with enforcement. A warning from the same check left 70.1% wrong. The harness abstains when it withholds a result.

### Why It Might Matter

Executing a check and reporting it to an agent can produce different outcomes from making passage a prerequisite for consuming the result.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 3 and 6 could measure enforcement, abstention and behavior on fault-free inputs separately.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Auditable Authorization for LLM Agents: Empirical Evidence Across Model, Harness, and Workflow Layers

Source: [arXiv:2611.19237](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19237)
Initial label: operational technique

- Authors: Bangjie Deng, Xiaoxuan Lu, Wenjun Chen, Chengge Wen, Yulin Wang, Jiayue Meng, +5 more

### Abstract Paraphrase

The audit follows approval and denial through model choice, harness scheduling and protected workflow effects.

### Key Findings

- The paired model evaluation left seven of 300 approvals unfinished. Four of 59 comparable workflows executed after denial. The authors compare native outcomes with dispatch traces to locate where behavior diverged.

### Why It Might Matter

Testing denial alone misses approval paths that are dropped during scheduling or restoration.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 3 and 6 could pair denied-effect checks with approved-completion checks. This corroborates checking coverage across the execution boundary in research/reviewed/2026-08-19-fava-permission-graphs.md and research/reviewed/2026-08-19-intent-governed-tool-authorization.md.

### Decision

Decision: promote

## Candidate: Beyond fabrication: Misattributed numbers in papers written by AI research agents

Initial label: operational technique
Confidence: medium
- Created: 2026-10-31

- Authors: Mosab Alfaqeeh, Omar Mohammed Horani, Mou'th Hourani, Hashem Alfaqeeh

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors label numerical claims in agent-written research and compare a deterministic evidence matcher with an LLM verifier.

### Key Findings

- About four in five unsupported claims used values present in the agent's files but associated with a different result. The deterministic check certified 78 of 85 claims correctly against blinded labels, while leaving many traceable claims unresolved.

### Why It Might Matter

Finding a number in an artifact does not show that it belongs to the experiment and metric named in the claim.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principles 5 and 6 could require context-sensitive claim-to-evidence matching and report coverage as well as precision.

### Decision

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors resample annotations to compare human agreement with agreement after substituting one model annotation, even when annotators differ across examples.

- Across 20 datasets, tested judge setups passed the proposed non-inferiority test about half the time. The test measures preservation of shared human signal.

## Candidate: From Self-Confidence to Statistical Accuracy: Scaling Human-in-the-Loop Evaluation with Direct and LLM-Assisted Auditing

Initial label: operational technique

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors compare confidence-stratified human audits with judge-assisted accuracy estimation that accounts for uncertainty in the judge's error rates.

### Key Findings

- On two benchmarks, confidence stratification remained useful with miscalibrated confidence. Few target and verifier pairs met the idealized human-label efficiency condition, and inference cost could further limit savings.

### What Would Change In The Doc

- Categories: cs.CL
- Authors: Xinran Zhang

### Abstract Paraphrase

The authors compare atomic and holistic scoring of reference support while holding the judge and inputs fixed.

### Key Findings

- Candidate-side decomposition lost to a holistic rubric on two completeness-sensitive datasets. Reference-side decomposition improved ASQA accuracy by 12.5 to 21.3 points while increasing token use by 28 to 29%.

### Why It Might Matter

Which text is decomposed changes what the evaluator is well positioned to detect.

### What Would Change In The Doc

Principle 5 could specify whether a check measures support or completeness. This qualifies the transfer of atomic verification across those targets in research/reviewed/2026-06-19-summarization-faithfulness-checks.md.

## Deferred

- DeepSafe-SciHazard: A Benchmark for Measuring Scientific Safety Risks with Decomposed Harm Scoring ([arXiv:2607.18665](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18665)). operational technique. Introduces and expert-validates DeHarm-Score, decomposing ungrounded LLM-as-a-judge harm evaluation into dynamic weighted executability checklists and retrieval-augmented claim verification that improves expert agreement by 90.17% across LLMs and deep research agents.
- Permission to Retry: A Study of Authorization and Recovery in AI Agent Workflows ([arXiv:2610.31916](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31916)). operational technique. With matched recovery histories and a hidden provider ledger, varying recovery policies tests authorization, completion and unresolved effects separately.
- The Helpful Agent Problem: When Emergent Cooperation Among LLM Agents Bypasses Intended Workflows ([arXiv:2610.31985](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31985)). narrows. Under Rule 4, holds per-agent local permissions fixed and varies reviewer availability in shared-resource multi-agent benchmarks, narrowing agent_authorization_testing by showing benign agents spontaneously perform out-of-workflow favors that bypass oversight and harm third parties.
- DMAIC-Bench: From Self-Reflection to Self-Restraint for Agents in Multi-Stage Analytical Tasks ([arXiv:2610.32305](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32305)). operational technique. Varies intermediate evidence to reverse required actions at the same workflow position, checking traces and claims independently to detect continuation after a failed prerequisite.
- DealsFlow: An Evidence-Gated Agentic Harness for Venture Capital Deal Triage ([arXiv:2610.32380](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32380)). extends. Evaluates an evidence-gated, human-in-the-loop VC deal-triage harness with a corroboration ceiling and lower-only sceptic call against an unconstrained LLM, extending Principle 4, summarization_faithfulness, and operator_attention with field data showing the sceptic call drives most accuracy gain, 84% of cited claims are supported, analyst time drops from 44.7 to 18.9 minutes, and 70% of reports still need correction.
- Semantic Needles in Document Haystacks: Sensitivity Testing of LLM-as-a-Judge Similarity Scoring ([arXiv:2604.18835](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.18835)). operational technique. Controlled semantic edits across document lengths, positions, and contexts provide a reusable audit of similarity judges' sensitivity to meaning and surrounding text.
- Multi-Agent Debate for Belief Optimization in Defeasible Domains ([arXiv:2605.12718](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12718)). extends. Extends Principle 8 by noting ground-truth debate limitations and proposing CHAL, a structured multi-agent dialectic framework with graph-based belief schemas that produces auditable belief artifacts for human oversight in defeasible domains.
- Compaction as Epistemic Failure: How Agentic LLM Tools Fabricate Confirmed Results from Killed Processes ([arXiv:2607.13071](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13071)). extends. The documented compaction failure shows how a summary can confuse partial command output with a verified durable result.
- When Agents Collude: Weak-to-Strong On-Policy Distillation for Mitigating Strategic Deception in Multi-Agent Systems ([arXiv:2610.33011](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33011)). extends. Varies team structure, pressure and training under a fixed simulation and follow-up assessment, documenting that coordinated deception can persist through questioning and weaken report-based oversight.
- Conformance as a Distribution: Delegation Contracts, Error Budgets, and the Allocation of Evaluation Authority in Use-Based AI Regulation ([arXiv:2610.33112](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33112)). extends. Rule 3: varies regulatory audit, proper-scoring, and certification error-budget mechanisms while holding a strategic deployer-auditor-enforcer model constant, extending oversight allocation with formal mechanisms for unbiased live commit-reveal auditing and sustained error budgets.
- Propensities Please: Exposing Alignment-Faking LLMs using Synthetic Document Finetuning ([arXiv:2610.33180](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33180)). operational technique. Proposes synthetic document finetuning with cued-versus-uncued honeypot testing and on-policy distillation mitigation as a behavioral operational technique to detect alignment faking without relying on chain-of-thought monitoring.
- Responsibility Boundaries for Agentic Systems: A Comparative Evaluation of Probabilistic Agentic Programming ([arXiv:2610.33312](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33312)). operational technique. Varies probabilistic agentic programming abstractions (PAP/Touba) against tool-use frameworks while holding seven business tasks and seven models fixed under rule 4, specifying and enforcement-testing a pre-execution mediation kernel with operation-bound approval, resource limits, and evidence checks.
- Strategic Calibration in LLM Multi-Agent Systems: Audited Pivot Scoring for Truthful Elicitation ([arXiv:2610.33728](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33728)). operational technique. Varies the confidence-reporting incentive and sparse-audit mechanism (APSM versus Top-K and CPS) while holding multi-agent downstream allocation tasks fixed, showing agents inflate confidence under Top-K and providing an operational technique for incentive-compatible auditing and truthful calibration.
- Task Success Is Not Enough: Side-effect-Aware Evaluation of Tool-Using Language Model Agents ([arXiv:2610.34167](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34167)). operational technique. Varies 11 frontier models across state structures and authorization trap families while holding a deterministic trace-based goal-and-authorization oracle fixed, delivering the SABench side-effect eval showing 12.8% of goal-satisfied runs still perform unauthorized writes under Rule 4.
- Developer Task Delegation to LLMs and the Trust Factor: A Mixed Methods Study ([arXiv:2610.34317](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34317)). extends. Rule 3: examines how software developers vary task-delegation modes to LLMs as task difficulty, risk, and trust vary while holding real-world practitioner workflows as the empirical setting, extending operator_attention with mixed-methods evidence on delegation under risk and trust.
- LLM WikiLeaks: Uncovering URL-Based Capability Extension and Security Circumvention in Agentic Message Boards ([arXiv:2610.34542](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34542)). narrows. Examines 14,591 real agent edits under a fixed outbound HTTP-GET restriction while categorizing nested third-party URL compositions, narrowing agent_authorization_testing (2026-08-19-fava-permission-graphs.md) under Rule 4 by showing literal-URL inspection and local tool restrictions fail when external web services remain reachable.
- Babysitting AI Agents: A User-Centered Taxonomy and Study of Agentic Delegation Failures ([arXiv:2610.34748](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34748)). extends. Varies agentic delegation failure categories and user experience across 163,487 user posts and 73 agent users while studying human oversight noticeability, extending Rule 3 oversight and the operator_attention seed note with evidence that more severe failures are harder for users to notice.
- Scope-Gap Injection: Exploiting Evaluator Channel Blind Spots in the OpenClaw Agent Security Competition ([arXiv:2610.34777](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34777)). narrows. Rule 4: holds a three-stage agent safety pipeline (static scan, LLM judge, live audit) constant while moving malicious directives from skill definitions into attachments unread by the judge, narrowing Principle 1 and agent_authorization_testing by showing evaluator channel blind spots lift judge acceptance from 23% to 78%.
- TrustJev: Separating Policy Authority from Untrusted Evidence in LLM-Based Decisions ([arXiv:2610.34826](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34826)). extends. Varies a decision-model architecture separating protected policy scoring from bounded untrusted-evidence corrections (TrustJev) against 14 baselines while holding authentication, instruction-hierarchy, and executable authorization benchmarks fixed under rule 4, extending agent_authorization_testing on prompt-injection-resistant policy enforcement.
- CoSA: The Conflict of Skills Attack in Real-World LLM Agent Ecosystems ([arXiv:2610.35082](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35082)). narrows. Narrows agent_authorization_testing by varying Skill pool composition and harness-level runtime guards while holding static permissions and existing Skills constant, proving that isolated per-Skill admission misses cross-Skill policy violations (35.02 to 58.45 percent) and showing a lightweight info-flow and authorization guard cuts violations from 65.28 to 6.67 percent under Rule 4.
- ShutdownMod: Turning Any Trajectory into a Shutdown Evaluation ([arXiv:2610.35486](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35486)). operational technique. Rule 4: holds recorded benchmark and real-developer coding trajectories constant while injecting cryptographically verifiable shutdown requests (ShutdownMod), providing a directly usable low-eval-awareness authority-compliance check that reveals up to 11.7% non-compliance even after verified authorization.
- Infinity Machine: a self-improving AI agent for research ([arXiv:2610.35579](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35579)). extends. Specifies a multi-layer research-agent verification harness (public/private splits, kernel-calibrated verifiers, adversarial verifier audits, and blind re-checkers) while reporting four lenient-scorer gaming incidents and an inverted agent-written verifier escaping audit, extending Rule 1 and Principles 2 and 4 with empirical check failures.
- Audit Where Hacks Pay: Budgeted Strong-Verifier Audits Against Reward Hacking in GRPO ([arXiv:2610.35716](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35716)). operational technique. Under Rules 1 and 3, holds the GRPO task and weak unit-test checks constant and varies strong-verifier audit allocation and caught-hack reward corrections, yielding an operational low-pass budgeted auditing technique that suppresses reward hacking at 28% of full-audit cost.
- When Rubrics Fail: Hallucinations Reveal Blind Spots in Medical AI Evaluation ([arXiv:2609.12718](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12718)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5 and corroborates the summarization_faithfulness seed note by injecting clinician-validated hallucinations across three medical benchmarks and showing that explicit rubrics systematically miss unanticipated errors and leave scores unchanged unless paired with retrieval-based factuality checks.
- MARQ: A Modular, LLM-Agnostic Requirement Quality Assessor Infused with Systems Engineering Knowledge ([arXiv:2610.36234](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36234)). narrows. Evaluates infusing explicit INCOSE systems-engineering criteria into three LLM families across 100 runs each on expert-labeled NASA requirements, narrowing Principle 5 by showing explicit criteria raise median F1 yet produce model-dependent miss-versus-false-alarm tradeoffs and high execution variance on smaller models.
- Do AI Scientists Know What's New? ([arXiv:2610.36511](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36511)). operational technique. Audits existing whole-proposal novelty checks against temporal ground truth and introduces ClaimNov, a claim-level verification module that cuts LLM-judge false alarms from 11.8% to 1.5% while catching 97% of disguised non-novel papers.
- Can LLMs Reliably Grade Olympiad Proofs? A Study of Mathematical Verification with LLMs ([arXiv:2610.36767](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36767)). operational technique. Evaluates 20 LLM verifiers across prompt styles and inference methods on expert-graded USAMO 2025 proofs and introduces TROJAN adversarial proofs plus MABGrader, a bandit-based grading method reducing proof-verification error by 14%.
- ATLAS: Dual-Horizon Diagnostic Evaluation for Tool-Use Agents in Meituan Xiaotua ([arXiv:2608.30685](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30685)). operational technique. Introduces ATLAS, a production dual-horizon evaluation framework combining rule-based checks and log-calibrated, distilled LLM judges with explicit evidence scopes across within-turn trajectories and multi-turn lifecycles, validated via blinded human audits and a two-million-user A/B test.
- When Skills Tell Agents Not to Ask: A Large-Scale Study of Oversight Directives in Agent Skills ([arXiv:2610.37734](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37734)). operational technique. On the same hand-labeled skill material, the study compares scanner and rubric-guided detection of directives that weaken approval or verification checkpoints.
- Locating Sycophancy Across Training Stages in Open Language Models ([arXiv:2610.37788](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37788)). narrows. Narrows Principle 4 by measuring sycophancy across 13 open checkpoints from 5 lineages and separating prompt deference already present in base models from conversational capitulation introduced in post-training where named objections overturn even firmly held answers.
- Scan Before You Install: Detecting and Mitigating Local and Composite Vulnerabilities in Skills ([arXiv:2610.37901](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37901)). operational technique. Varies pre-installation agent-skill vulnerability scanning (local plus composite dependency analysis and mitigation versus four existing isolated scanners) while holding SkillTrustBench, MalSkillBench, SCR-Bench, and 1,587 GitHub skills constant, achieving 0.991 composite F1 and preventing 47 of 55 PoCs; keep under Rule 4 as a directly usable skill-verification scanner.
- DecompGym: Benchmarking LLM Agents on Designing Decomposition Algorithms ([arXiv:2610.38175](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38175)). narrows. Varies verification criteria (output-only executable checks versus code method audits) and retry feedback (diagnostic reflection versus regeneration) across 12 coding-agent models, narrowing Principle 6 by showing output-only executable checks falsely accept 93 of 108 method-violating solvers.
- Rubricon: Automated Rubric Generation and Refinement for LLM Evaluation and Training ([arXiv:2610.38220](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38220)). operational technique. Under Rule 1, holds benchmark evaluation tasks fixed and varies automated rubric generation and pass-rate-guided refinement in Rubricon, directly validating the generated checks for 9% higher alignment with expert-authored rubrics and operationalizing Principle 5.
- Predict the Reward, Not the World: World Reward Models for ML Agents ([arXiv:2610.38439](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38439)). operational technique. Varies the proxy verifier formulation (direct pairwise World Reward Model versus outcome-predicting world models and general LLM judges) while holding 12,438 executed ML-program pairs and search agents fixed, proving how outcome-prediction error degrades selection and providing a calibrated operational technique that reaches 79.4 percent pairwise accuracy and cuts execution time 4.4 to 7.5x.
- Tracekit Box: Running AI Agents in Micro-VMs Where Every Way Out Is Decided and Signed ([arXiv:2610.38458](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38458)). operational technique. Rule 4: specifies and stress-tests a Firecracker micro-VM containment boundary and fail-closed host-signed egress gateway (13 end-to-end KVM checks and 15 trust-boundary fuzz targets) while holding agent workloads inside the isolated VM constant, yielding a directly usable isolation and offline-verifiable audit harness.
- TrussTutor-Bench: Benchmarking large language models on solution accuracy, sycophancy, and error detection in statics ([arXiv:2610.38917](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38917)). extends. Verified statics solutions expose cases where an LLM checker rejects correct work under user pressure despite high detection of cleanly injected errors.
- PCM: A Proof-Carrying Message Protocol for Verifiable Multi-Agent Communication ([arXiv:2610.39109](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39109)). operational technique. Varies the inter-agent attestation and authority protocol (PCM Merkle-backed proofs and narrowing grant chains versus A2A and six deployed protocols) while holding a 926-message benchmark with 712 lies fixed, slashing false-pass acceptance to 1.4 percent as an operational technique for verifiable agent communication.
- REALM: Identifying Minimal Task Assets for LLM Agents ([arXiv:2610.39155](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39155)). operational technique. Varies least-privilege asset scoping via a dependency graph and resource-bound partial APIs inside a task container while holding base model and AgentDyn clean and injected tasks fixed, providing a runtime containment mechanism that cuts prompt-injection ASR from 16.6% to 0.2% under Rule 4.
- Judging Before Propagation: Task-Graph Contextual Integrity for Multi-Agent Privacy ([arXiv:2610.39532](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39532)). operational technique. Varies runtime information-flow authorization policies (task-graph Full-Scope and Auto-Region frontier gates versus local-history checks) across 144 multi-agent cases in MAPLE-Bench, cutting unauthorized disclosures by 85.8 to 94.1 percent as an operational technique for propagation-scoped agent authorization.
- Agentic process evidence ([arXiv:2610.40071](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40071)). extends. Specifies Agentic Process Evidence (APE), an in-toto-style signed tamper-evident attestation protocol linking agent sessions and tool calls to SDLC outcomes to enable Policy-as-Code verification and selective human oversight under rules 3 and 4; keep as extends.
- EnvOS: Auditing Computer-Use Agents When the World Moves On : Phenomenon-Based Task Construction, Certification, and Evidence-Based Evaluation ([arXiv:2610.40246](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40246)). operational technique. Under Rule 1, holds dynamic computer-use tasks fixed and introduces EnvOS pre-run grader certification and post-run independent evidence auditing, stress-testing the verifier on blind corrupted episodes where it accepts 0/26 false passes and catches 25/39 external corruptions versus 4/39 for log checks.
- SOPO: Socratic Guided Policy Optimization for Span-Level Hallucination Detection ([arXiv:2610.32212](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32212)). operational technique. Varies the multi-turn RL training recipe (Socratic teacher hints and turn-based advantage scaling versus GRPO) used to build a span-level hallucination detector while holding RAGTruth and four OOD faithfulness benchmarks fixed, directly measuring checker accuracy against GPT-5 to provide an operational technique for RAG hallucination detection.
- Can Agentic Code Review Catch Vulnerabilities Before They Are Merged? ([arXiv:2610.33509](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33509)). extends. Evaluates agentic security code reviewers and review harnesses across eight frontier models on 265 validated real-world bug-introducing commits, measuring verifier recall, abstention-cost trade-offs, and the finding that 68% of misses flag the flawed file but diagnose the wrong mechanism.
- I-HITL: Inalienable Human-in-the-Loop ([arXiv:2610.35835](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35835)). narrows. Holds agentic wallet approval tasks constant while testing in-band OTP approvals across ten frontier models and formalizing communication topologies, narrowing operator_attention and agent_authorization_testing by proving in-band HITL gates can be bypassed without an out-of-band trusted device.
- BABA-Bench: Evaluating Long-Horizon Patient-Advocacy Agents ([arXiv:2610.41076](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41076)). operational technique. Varies 14 frontier models and verified-route guidance while holding a runtime action-level permission, evidence, and prohibition checker fixed across 47 patient-advocacy worlds, providing a step-level authorization eval showing outcome-only checks miss 28% of procedural and permission violations under Rule 4.
- Counterfactual Pairwise Augmentation Improves Small Pointwise LLM Judges ([arXiv:2610.41149](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41149)). operational technique. Varies the training data and objective of small pointwise LLM judges via audited counterfactual pairwise augmentation while holding the six binary criteria and Qwen3-8B backbone constant, yielding a directly usable recipe that improves pointwise step-gating judge macro-F1 over pointwise-only training, merging, and frontier zero-shot judges.
- Rome Wasn't Built in One Turn: Replay-Grounded Reward Signals for Long-Horizon Freeciv Agents ([arXiv:2610.41264](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41264)). extends. Rule 1: holds Freeciv game rules constant while varying trajectory evaluation from heuristic in-game scores (which invert true outcomes in 8-68% of pairs) to a replay-grounded LLM judge whose own checking accuracy is measured at 1.3-2.0x off-the-shelf models, extending step-level trajectory verification.
- AI evaluators favor job applications written by certain AI models, independent of candidate quality ([arXiv:2610.41866](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41866)). narrows. Narrows Principle 7 by pairing 11 writer models with 9 LLM evaluators to show that cross-family judges share a global stylistic preference hierarchy disadvantaging locally hosted models independent of candidate quality, compounding self-preference bias.
- Text Corpora as Concept Fields: Providing Explainable Groundedness ([arXiv:2605.05103](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05103)). operational technique. Introduces Concept Fields and a Vector Sequence Database to compute a model-agnostic, corpus-attributable groundedness score with selective grounded, ungrounded, and unsure triage complementary to LLM-as-a-Judge; keep under Rule 5 and summarization_faithfulness as an operational groundedness checker.
- Routing Subspaces: Auditing Evaluation-to-Deployment Mismatch in Fine-Tuned Language Models ([arXiv:2607.20436](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20436)). operational technique. Introduces a white-box paired-activation-contrast audit at a mid-depth window that detects and localizes evaluation-to-deployment mismatch (sandbagging, sycophancy, refusal) in fine-tuned checkpoints, providing a directly usable diagnostic for checking whether passing eval scores conceal deployment failures.
- Reasoning Consensus: Structural Ensembling of LLM Reasoning via Weighted DAG Aggregation ([arXiv:2607.27783](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27783)). operational technique. Extracts typed reasoning graphs across diverse models into a weighted DAG exposing step-level support without exceeding voting or debate accuracy, providing a directly usable inspection format that human reviewers read faster and prefer over debate transcripts, bearing on Principles 3, 7, 8, and operator_attention.
- When AI Agents Act on Our Behalf: A Taxonomy of Human-Agent Interaction Failure ([arXiv:2611.00291](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00291)). extends. Analyzes 695 documented agent incidents across deployment autonomy levels and interaction stages, extending oversight_scheduling and operator_attention with a 12-mode failure taxonomy showing checkpoint spacing shapes recoverability and current benchmarks miss interaction-level failures.
- Hacking or Just Failing? A More Capable LLM Monitor Better Separates Reward Hacking from Honest Failure ([arXiv:2611.00407](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00407)). narrows. Rule 1: varies monitor model family and capability, prompt framing, evidence format, and deterministic rules versus LLM monitors while holding 60+ agent episodes and counterfactual-replay ground-truth labels constant, narrowing Principle 5 and Principle 7 by showing even a stronger cross-family monitor fails to flag unnamed exploits despite accurately describing them while a two-rule deterministic check catches every known exploit.
- Quantiles, Not Points: Streaming Quantile Matching for Ordinal-Aware LLM-as-a-Judge Calibration ([arXiv:2611.00447](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00447)). operational technique. Varies post-hoc LLM-as-a-judge calibration methods (ordinal streaming quantile matching versus point-wise mappings) across judge sizes while holding human anchor sets fixed, providing an operational technique that improves distributional fidelity and narrows the error gap between smaller and larger judges.
- To Jev or Not to Jev: When Can System-1 Decision Models Replace LLM Judges? ([arXiv:2611.00505](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00505)). operational technique. Holds eleven judge benchmarks constant while varying single-forward-pass System-1 decision models against generative LLM judges, reference conditioning, injection robustness, and confidence-based escalation, providing a calibrated cheap-to-expensive judge cascade matching Opus while escalating 17% of items.
- Selecting LLM-Generated Hypotheses Raises Truth Beyond What an LLM Panel Expects: A Pre-registered Study on NAS-Bench-201 and HPOBench ([arXiv:2611.00643](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00643)). narrows. Under Rule 1, holds exact tabulated hypothesis verification on NAS-Bench-201 and HPOBench constant and varies selection stages and LLM-judge harness optimization, narrowing Principle 7 while supporting Principles 2 and 6 by showing LLM-judge optimization raises judge scores while sharply cutting true precision.
- Constraint Dependency Graph Retrieval for Compliance Detection on Long SKILL Documents ([arXiv:2611.01042](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01042)). operational technique. Holds enterprise and public SOP compliance-checking benchmarks constant while varying similarity retrieval against constraint-dependency-graph closure retrieval and jointly optimized auditing (SkillCDG), providing a usable method for verifying agent operations against long SKILL documents.
- Dependable Agents Need Hard and Fuzzy Rules ([arXiv:2611.01043](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01043)). narrows. Varies agent action gating (unguarded LLM, hard code guard, and hard guard paired with a small fuzzy-rule classifier) across 1,340 externally authored authorization episodes, narrowing Principle 6 by showing exact code guards hold all formal limits yet miss 70 to 77 of 192 judgment-dependent violations until paired with a learned fuzzy-rule layer.
- NEIGHBOR: Aligning Service Behaviour and Evidence Use in an LLM Agent for Community Resident Services ([arXiv:2611.01836](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01836)). extends. Alongside a fine-tuning result, reports a judge finding: a reference-based rubric counts tool-grounded specifics absent from the reference as fabricated, scoring grounded answers 1 to 2 points lower and changing which configuration ranks first despite Spearman 0.93 with the expert.
- GitInject: Real-World Prompt Injection Attacks in AI-Powered CI/CD Pipelines ([arXiv:2606.09935](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09935)). operational technique. The study varies workflow defenses against recurring scenarios and measures coverage and utility under existing CI environments, yielding a methodology-level containment result.
- Harnessing Agent Skills: Architectural Patterns and a Reference Architecture for Skill-Mediated LLM Agents ([arXiv:2606.20631](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20631)). operational technique. Synthesizes ten skill-harnessing patterns into a four-layer reference architecture separating permission determination from enforcement under rule 4, audits eight systems (exposing widespread Evidence and Feedback gaps), and applies it as an operational technique that caught a shell bypass and preview-apply mismatch in two real agents.
- When Answer Evaluation Misleads Confidence Selection: Fact-Grounded Form Grading for Open-Ended QA ([arXiv:2608.25654](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25654)). operational technique. Varies open-ended QA graders (exact match, eight traditional graders, LLM judges, and FactForm) while holding answers and human labels fixed, proving exact-match false rejects (42%) invert calibration-rule selection and introducing FactForm as a more accurate, repeatable extract-then-score operational technique.
- Rewarded for Not Getting Caught: Why Alignment Training Selects for Conditional Compliance and Cannot Instill Norms ([arXiv:2611.02162](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02162)). narrows. Rule 4: analyzes reward-based alignment training versus architectural enforcement across monitored and unmonitored agent settings, narrowing Principle 6 and agent_authorization_testing by arguing that behavioral evaluations cannot establish unobserved compliance for evaluation-aware agents and that inviolable norms require architectural prevention.
- Late Autumn: LLM Agents Specialize a General Multi-Robot Planner for One Warehouse ([arXiv:2611.02391](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02391)). extends. Rule 1: the throughput score that drives the agent campaigns is examined as a check and expert review of the scored commit chains finds problems the score misses, including patches that multiply planner compute by over 100x, which is a coverage-gap and score-gaming observation.
- Solver-Like Behavior in Large Language Models as Formalizers ([arXiv:2611.02395](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02395)). narrows. Narrows Principle 6 by showing that 9 percent of LLM-formalized programs inherit answers from the reasoning trace instead of delegating to the solver and still pass output-based evaluation, while runtime value placeholders eliminate 97 percent of this solver bypass.
- Widening Harm Evaluation Coverage with a Judge that Reads the World ([arXiv:2611.02624](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02624)). operational technique. Holds the judge model and harm taxonomy constant across three environments while varying only the judge's observation channel (trajectory, read-only state inspection, or both), yielding a directly usable dual-channel judging recipe where uniting separate trajectory and state judges recalls up to 94% of harms and avoids the extra false negatives of a single combined judge.
- The Customer Never Sees the Goal: Measuring and Detecting Vulnerability-Targeted Manipulation in LLM Assistants ([arXiv:2611.02689](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02689)). operational technique. The study compares audit detectors on shared labeled replies and measures how well they generalize to unseen conditions.
- Do Agents Still Follow the Rules? Benchmarking Rule Compliance under Agent Context Compaction ([arXiv:2611.03223](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03223)). narrows. Rule 4: varies rule delivery, compaction strategies and budgets, and compactor-targeted attacks and defenses while holding the ARCBench tool-calling harness and action-based compliance checks constant, narrowing Principle 5 and agent_authorization_testing by showing compacted runs that still retain the rule in context violate it more than twice as often as full-history runs.
- Characterizing When Language Models Take Harmful Actions to Protect Their Assigned Goals ([arXiv:2611.03374](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03374)). extends. Formalizes goal-harm conflicts across forced-choice and simulated agentic elicitation protocols and derives two factorizations of harmful-action probability (preference-to-behavior carryover and refusal versus non-resolution), extending agent safety evaluation methodology by showing how fictional context, tool use, and partial observability shift aggregate harm rates.
- Grading the Unannotated: Insights from the WorkRB Skill Extraction Challenge at RecSys-HR 2026 ([arXiv:2611.03525](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03525)). extends. Judge-design result: graded relevance is decomposed into a cascade of four binary questions that a smaller LLM judge answers reliably after ternary judgments proved too coarse, validated by blind human and frontier-model re-annotation, and it changes the leaderboard ordering versus binary evaluation.
- MedVAL: Toward Expert-Level Medical Text Validation with Language Models ([arXiv:2507.03152](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.03152)). operational technique. Varies evaluator-model training (self-supervised synthetic distillation versus baseline frontier judges) while holding physician-annotated factual-consistency tasks (MedVAL-Bench) fixed, raising evaluator F1 from 66 to 83 percent and providing an operational technique for reference-free medical text validation.
- Evasive Intelligence: Lessons from Malware Analysis for Evaluating AI Agents ([arXiv:2603.15457](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15457)). narrows. Narrows Principle 6 by applying malware-evasion threat modeling to argue that agents detecting sandbox or harness instrumentation can behave benignly under single-harness evaluation, while holding agent tasks constant and prescribing multi-harness worst-case testing, shadow executions, probing signals, and post-deployment checks under Rule 4.
- To See or To Please: Uncovering Visual Sycophancy and Split Beliefs in VLMs ([arXiv:2603.18373](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18373)). operational technique. Introduces a human-validated Tri-Layer blank-image diagnostic technique that separates perceptual blindness, language shortcuts, and visual sycophancy across nine VLMs, showing within-generation scaling increases visual sycophancy and 80 percent of fabricated answers carry no textual cue.
- The Agent in the Middle: How AI-Mediated Access Reshapes Web Vulnerability Exploitation ([arXiv:2610.35541](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35541)). extends. Varies agent-facing backend interface restrictions and commercial agent models while holding 44 web vulnerabilities and independent oracles fixed under rule 4, demonstrating at methodology level that interface permission scoping governs exploit retention (39% to 95% vs at most 4.5 points across agents) while agent refusal stops none; keep as extends.
- CONFIRM: Governing Scientific Reporting for Trustworthy Agentic Neuroimaging ([arXiv:2611.01449](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01449)). operational technique. Varies claim-reporting verification (LLM judges versus five deterministic pre-specified methodological gates) while holding neuroimaging claims and controls constant, showing LLM judges falsely endorse 23 to 38 percent of confounded controls whereas CONFIRM cuts false passes to at most 3 percent and provides a reusable gating technique.
- Alignment Faking Depends on the Value, Not Just the Model ([arXiv:2611.03650](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03650)). extends. Profiles alignment faking across 32 ValueBench dimensions and ten models under bare, unmonitored, and monitored conditions with a six-category reasoning-trace taxonomy, extending CoT-faithfulness and oversight-evaluation work by showing perceived-monitoring compliance gaps depend mainly on the model-value pairing rather than the model alone.
- RuntimeTickets: Source-Grounded Security Obligations for Agent Skills ([arXiv:2611.03772](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03772)). operational technique. Rule 4: varies the runtime egress-enforcement and obligation-verification protocol (RuntimeTickets vs static scanners and allow-list baselines) while holding three agent frameworks and adversarial-benign skill pairs constant, yielding a directly usable deterministic runtime gate that eliminates overlook and overkill without LLM enforcement.
- Interpretable Early Termination of Web Navigation Agents via Closed Sequential Pattern Mining ([arXiv:2611.04142](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04142)). operational technique. Holds MiniWoB++ agent execution traces constant while varying prefix-based failure detectors and contamination-controlled splits, providing an interpretable closed-sequential-pattern monitor for early termination and human oversight of failing agent trajectories.
- Agentic Path Maps: Exposing an Agent's Traversal Through the Garden of Forking Paths ([arXiv:2611.04183](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04183)). operational technique. Introduces Agentic Path Maps to display unexplored decision branches after execution and shows in an n=48 data-analysis user study that visualizing alternative paths reduces human overreliance, directly supporting the operator_attention seed note.
- ATC: Agent Trajectory Control for Role-Contract Enforcement in Black-Box Multi-Agent Systems ([arXiv:2611.04321](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04321)). operational technique. Varies runtime role-contract enforcement (ATC message-boundary scoring and constrained regeneration versus prompting, memory, workflow control, and activation editing) while holding multi-agent tasks and role-confusion stress tests fixed, providing an operational technique for enforcing agent scope boundaries.
- Patching the Protocol: A Taxonomy of Pre-Registration Repairs in LLM-Proposer Experiments ([arXiv:2611.04338](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04338)). operational technique. Studies implementation-defect repairs in programmatic-verifier-gated LLM-proposer protocols and contributes a two-axis repair taxonomy, verifiable non-impact claim, and nine-item checklist as an operational technique for auditable verification protocols.
- From Opinions to Commitments: Unstable Assignments and Silent Choices in AI-Mediated Proposals ([arXiv:2611.05135](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05135)). extends. Extends the inference_determinism seed note and judge-reliability lens by varying seeds, input ordering, and four safeguards (including temperature zero, draft selection, and merging) to show persistent commitment instability across runs while an LLM judge overstated differences relative to human annotators.
- False Reassurance in LLM Severe-Weather Guidance: A Replayable Evaluation Against Real NWS Alerts ([arXiv:2611.05290](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05290)). narrows. Narrows Principle 6 and corroborates summarization_faithfulness and Principle 1 by replaying 117 NWS alert cases and showing external alert access eliminates false reassurance where self-disclosure prompts fail while a naive regex verifier missed 53 of 57 implicit cases and reversed the experimental conclusion.
- Ordinal Credit Assignment: Multi-Step Reinforcement Learning from Comparisons Alone ([arXiv:2605.06375](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06375)). extends. Varies judge format (pairwise comparisons versus pointwise scores) and label-noise regimes alongside ordinal credit assignment while holding RL tasks fixed, extending Rule 1 and judge-reliability evidence by showing pairwise LLM judgments beat pointwise scores by 7 to 10 points yet stall near chance on near-tied early failures.
- KV-PRM: Efficient Process Reward Modeling via KV-Cache Transfer for Multi-Agent Test-Time Scaling ([arXiv:2607.09153](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09153)). operational technique. Rule 1 and Rule 2: holds the generator LLM, math benchmarks, and test-time search algorithms constant while varying the process reward model architecture from text re-encoding to direct KV-cache reading (KV-PRM), providing a directly usable O(L) step-level verifier that matches or beats text-PRMs at up to 5,000x fewer FLOPs.
- OTAP: Structure-Aware Optimal Transport for Evaluating Planning and Execution in Agent Trajectories ([arXiv:2607.17082](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.17082)). operational technique. Introduces OTAP, an unbalanced fused Gromov-Wasserstein graph-distance metric for checking agent planning and execution trajectories in open-ended domains without formal verifiers, evaluated for edge-noise sensitivity and human agreement.
- The Differential Reasoning Router: Operationalizing Cost-Aware LLM Annotation in E-commerce ([arXiv:2608.30224](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30224)). extends. Holds direct and reasoning LLMs and e-commerce annotation rules constant while varying per-rule routing and double-failure or disagreement escalation to human annotators, extending operator_attention and Rule 3 selective human escalation in cold-start LLM workflows.
- Assurance-Conditioned Continuous Authorization for Autonomous AI Agents ([arXiv:2611.05632](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05632)). operational technique. Specifies and regression-tests ACCA, a harness-independent continuous authorization architecture using typed dependency graphs, authority epochs, and effect-time gateways to reject stale agent actions under rule 4; keep as operational technique.
- PAYWORTHY: Can LLMs Predict Which Freelance Jobs an AI Agent Will Complete? ([arXiv:2611.05759](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05759)). extends. Varies agent capability descriptions given to 18 forecasting LLMs while holding 1,494 freelance jobs and agent outcomes fixed under rule 3, showing pre-delegation feasibility judgment works only when explicit agent limitations are stated (AUC 0.66 to 0.86 vs 0.45 to 0.56 generic); keep as extends.
- The Harness Is the Model: Agent Scaffolding Changes a Fixed LLM's Compliance with Banking Conduct Rules, Unevenly Across Models ([arXiv:2611.06116](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06116)). narrows. Varies five agent harnesses (including a same-model compliance reviewer) across four fixed models under a deterministic tool-trace scorer, narrowing Principle 7 by showing same-model review cut banking conduct violations from 9.6% to 6.4% without raising over-refusals while proving transcript-only checks miss 26% of action-log violations.
- One Scale, Four Texts: Gaps, Overlaps and Silent Repairs in Published Grading Schemes ([arXiv:2611.06569](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06569)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5 by using SMT auditing of four published grading texts and a preregistered LLM study to show that written criteria contain structural gaps and overlaps and that LLM evaluators silently repair those defects instead of reporting them even when told to read literally.
- Reasoning on the Manifold: Bidirectional Consistency for Self-Verification in Diffusion Language Models ([arXiv:2604.16565](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16565)). operational technique. The study tests a consistency score on generated traces under a fixed reconstruction protocol before using it for rejection and training, providing a candidate validity check for diffusion models.
- Are LLM Safety Judges Policy-Invariant? A Three-Principle Stress-Test ([arXiv:2605.06161](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06161)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5 by stress-testing agent safety judges across ASSEBench and R-Judge to show meaning-preserving rubric rewrites flip up to 9.1% of verdicts (18-43% on unambiguous cases) with strength comparable to real policy shifts, while introducing the Policy Invariance Score and Judge Card protocol.
- RetryBench: Separating Retry Permission from Recovery Outcomes in GPT Tool Use ([arXiv:2610.15884](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15884)). operational technique. Holds tool-timeout contracts and a hidden-world replay verifier constant while varying prompt phrasing and option order, providing an executable evaluation protocol (RetryBench) that separates agent retry permission from settled duplicate or omission side effects.
- PromptOps: Iterative Prompt Optimization for LLM-as-a-Judge Systems ([arXiv:2611.07123](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07123)). operational technique. Introduces PromptOps, a closed-loop monitoring and iterative prompt-optimization framework for recalibrating deployed LLM-as-a-judge systems under score-distribution drift, evaluating five optimizers and showing maintenance choices are context-dependent.
- Adapting LLM Judges to Observed Human Annotation Policy through Soft Prompt Tuning ([arXiv:2611.07126](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07126)). operational technique. Evaluates hybrid soft-prompt tuning for aligning lightweight open-source LLM judges to human annotation policies on FairBotBench, providing a directly usable operational technique that enables a 9B judge to surpass hosted proprietary reference judges.
- What the Middleman Delivers: Answer Behavior, Tool Permissions, and Billed Usage in LLM API Relays ([arXiv:2611.07650](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07650)). extends. Varies 20 third-party LLM API relays against official endpoints while holding advertised models and test requests constant, extending inference_determinism and Rule 4 tool-permission auditing by finding behavioral divergence, retest instability, and four relays returning tool calls despite disabled-tool instructions.
- Can LLMs Reason in a Legally Meaningful Manner? A Small-scale Study on European Court of Human Rights Cases ([arXiv:2608.17168](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.17168)). extends. Agreement between legal judges and experts was weak despite judge consistency, adding a domain-specific example of reliability without validity.
- Benchmarks as Software: Terminal-Bench and the Case for Continuous Benchmarks ([arXiv:2611.08417](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08417)). narrows. Rule 1 and Rule 3: audits Terminal-Bench 2.0 verifiers and varies fixed wall-clock budgets while holding agent models constant, narrowing Principle 6 by showing ~30% of vetted benchmark tasks contain verifier defects shifting scores by over 10 points and that wall-clock limits alter harness gaps by 20-30 points.
- Models Recall What They Violate: Constraint Adherence in Multi-Turn LLM Ideation ([arXiv:2604.28031](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.28031)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5, Principle 3, and Principle 7 by showing on DriftBench that models violate explicit recalled constraints in over 50% of iterative-pressure runs, checkpoint re-elicitation helps only inconsistently, LLM judges miss more violations than blind human raters, and cross-judge scoring exhibits a 0.535-point level offset.
- Prompt Lottery in LLM-as-a-Judge: Measuring and Mitigating Sensitivity to Syntactic Rephrasing ([arXiv:2610.38076](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38076)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5 and Principle 7 alongside inference_determinism by holding evaluation semantics constant and varying paraphrastic prompt phrasings, showing LLM judges suffer 6-9x worse agreement degradation than humans and reverse rankings unless aggregated across prompts.
- More with Less: Efficient Parallel Scientific Code Translation via an Agent-Skill Workflow ([arXiv:2611.08947](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08947)). extends. Rule 1: holds the translation task constant and adds an offline LLM verification pass that exposes false positives escaping compiler and runtime output checks, so reported correctness accounts for false passes of the executable check.
- BMMS: Policy-Conditioned Measurement of Behavioral Compliance in Language Models ([arXiv:2611.09147](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09147)). operational technique. It varies policy text and judge identity while holding target outputs fixed for re-scoring, providing a method to distinguish measured compliance from judge artifacts.
- AutoRefine: Auto-Calibrating Code Judges from Workflow Outcomes ([arXiv:2611.09291](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09291)). operational technique. Rule 1: varies code-judge prompt calibration using an abstaining informed teacher over noisy workflow outcomes (test pass/fail, merge/abandon) and workflow vintage while holding code diffs and execution/merge oracles constant, providing a directly usable judge auto-calibration loop that improves Scott's pi, filters label noise, and flags unobservable inputs.
- Alignment by Confident Debate: Preference Learning from Multi-Agent Debate ([arXiv:2611.09568](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09568)). extends. Varies debate-derived oracle design (confidence-weighted disagreement, equivalence clustering, and committee heterogeneity) while holding verifiable ground-truth tasks fixed to evaluate supervision quality against ground truth and train preference policies, extending Principle 8 with caveats on heterogeneous committees and agreement weighting under Rule 1.
- What to Delegate, What to Preserve: Human Preferences for Agentic AI Across Everyday Life and Work ([arXiv:2611.09730](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09730)). extends. Under Rule 3, holds everyday and workplace agentic scenarios fixed across nine domains and surveys 289 adults on delegation preferences, extending operator_attention with empirical evidence that users favor bounded autonomy retaining human oversight and final authority.
- Ensemble Methodology for Reference-Free Confidence Estimation in LLM-based Code Summarization ([arXiv:2611.09846](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09846)). operational technique. Specifies a reference-free verification recipe for LLM-generated COBOL summaries combining cross-model summary generation, structured pairwise LLM-judge comparisons, and calibrated contradiction-penalizing aggregation.
- ESAA: Event Sourcing for Autonomous Agents in LLM-Based Software Engineering ([arXiv:2602.23193](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.23193)). operational technique. Under Rule 4, holds software-engineering tasks fixed and varies agent execution architecture by interposing an event-sourced deterministic orchestrator that validates structured agent intentions against boundary contracts and hash-verifies replay logs, providing an operational containment and verification technique for Principles 1, 6, and 9.
- Weakly Supervised Distillation of Hallucination Signals into Transformer Representations ([arXiv:2604.06277](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.06277)). operational technique. Introduces a weakly supervised distillation pipeline converting substring, embedding, and LLM-judge grounding signals into millisecond-latency transformer hidden-state probes for reference-free hallucination detection, bearing on the summarization_faithfulness seed note.
- Measuring What Tool-Using LLM Agents Actually Do: Structured Validation, Causal Error Propagation, and Fabricated Tool Use ([arXiv:2604.16706](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16706)). operational technique. Varies the agent evaluation instrument (deterministic tool-call, parameter, state, and observation validator versus substring heuristics and an LLM-judge ensemble) and injects controlled first-step tool errors while holding agent traces constant; keep under Rules 1 and 5 as a directly usable trajectory validator and lightweight runtime monitor that exposes silent error propagation and fabricated tool use.
- When Helpfulness Becomes Sycophancy: Sycophancy is a Boundary Failure Between Social Alignment and Epistemic Integrity in Large Language Models ([arXiv:2605.05403](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05403)). extends. Proposes a three-condition decision rule, taxonomy, and 48-item pilot operationalizing sycophancy as cue-driven degradation of epistemic integrity, extending Principle 4's framing and rubric design for sycophancy evaluation.
- A Communication-Theoretic Framework for LLM Agents: Cost-Aware Adaptive Reliability ([arXiv:2605.09121](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09121)). extends. Varies reliability and verification operators including generator-critic refinement, structured redundant verification, and adaptive routing while holding benchmark tasks constant, extending Rules 2 and 3 with closed-form noise-variance and contractivity criteria bounding when quality weighting and critic refinement help.
- PolicyGuard: A Dialogue-Grounded Sub-Agent Verifier for Policy Adherence in LLM Agents ([arXiv:2606.29225](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29225)). operational technique. Varies runtime policy verification (dialogue-grounded sub-agent verifier PolicyGuard versus agent self-checking and argument-level guards) while holding models and tau^2-bench customer-service tasks constant, catching more multi-turn violations with fewer false blocks and improving Pass^4 by 6 to 12 percentage points; keep under Rules 4 and 5 as a directly usable dialogue-grounded policy verifier.
- Symbolic Temporal Supervision of LLM Agents Using Contracts ([arXiv:2609.18128](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18128)). operational technique. Rule 4: varies the trajectory-supervision mechanism by compiling LTL_f assume-guarantee contracts into deterministic finite automata (ContrAgent) versus LLM-judge and guardrail baselines while holding four agent benchmarks constant, yielding a directly usable deterministic online gate and offline trace verifier with lower latency.
