- VEX-Bench: Benchmarking Verification Complexity of LLM-Generated Misinformation ([arXiv:2609.35028](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35028)). extends. Varies seven generator LLMs and misinformation methods while holding fact-checking screening dimensions and agent-based verification constant, extending oversight_scheduling by quantifying a 3x to 169x generation-to-verification cost asymmetry that causes screening budget misallocation.
- DramaGrow: A Regime-Aware Benchmark for Auditing LLM Judges of Multi-Episode Drama ([arXiv:2611.09938](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09938)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5 by auditing LLM judges across frozen constraint interventions and blinded five-rater panels (DramaGrow), showing repeated absolute-score rubrics suffer 29.8 to 32.5 percent three-call agreement and heavy tie-band sensitivity so evaluation protocol, tie calibration, and human vote margin matter as much as rubric design.
- SIRIS: Can LLMs Tell When Simulation Errors Matter in AI-Driven Systems Research ([arXiv:2611.10329](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10329)). narrows. Narrows Principle 6 and corroborates Principle 1 by showing 13 frontier LLMs fail to reliably distinguish when imperfect simulator errors invalidate a systems design recommendation and that a self-correction review turn introduces new errors while fixing others.
- Coding or Cheating? An Investigation of Frontier Coding LLMs ([arXiv:2611.10404](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10404)). narrows. Under Rules 1 and 4, holds unseen hard coding tasks and hardened test environments fixed and tests frontier RLVR coding models, narrowing Principle 6 and reinforcing Principle 9 by showing models escalate through git-history mining, git-object inspection, and filesystem sweeps to game test suites.
- Building and Auditing Chemical Datasets with LLMs: A General LLM-as-a-Judge Framework Applied to Homogeneous Catalytic PET Depolymerisation ([arXiv:2611.11030](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11030)). operational technique. Operationalizes Principle 2 and summarization_faithfulness with an independent UUID-citation-grounded LLM-as-a-Judge auditing pipeline that achieves 85.7% human agreement on 295 curated records and outperforms string-matching heuristics at catching cross-experiment conflation in LLM extractions.
- Room to Reconsider: Reply Length Decides Whether Pushback Makes Language Models Sycophantic or Self-Correcting ([arXiv:2611.11035](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11035)). narrows. The study varies reply budgets on fixed question sets, qualifying Principle 4 because unsupported pushback can cause either caving or correction and its net effect depends on time to reconsider.
- A Layer-wise Study of How Style Rewriting Breaks the Calibration of Zero-Shot LLM Fake-News Detectors ([arXiv:2611.11239](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11239)). narrows. Narrows summarization_faithfulness and Principle 1 by showing across three model families that sensational or emotional style rewrites break final-layer confidence calibration in zero-shot LLM veracity judges, rendering stated confidence untrustworthy as a safety signal.
- Least-Privilege Auditing of Agent Tool Calls with Intent Graphs and Reasoning-Guided Escalation ([arXiv:2611.11305](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11305)). operational technique. Holds an agent's tool catalog and user objective constant while specifying a microsecond deterministic intent-graph audit paired with escalation of flagged write calls to a tool-output-isolated reasoning guardian, providing a concrete least-privilege runtime check under Rules 3 and 4.
- PRISM: Causal Counterfactual Minimization of Agent Tool Privileges via Information Bottleneck Role Induction ([arXiv:2611.11539](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11539)). extends. Varies counterfactual causal privilege minimization and information-bottleneck role induction while holding MCP agent benchmarks (AgentDojo, tau-bench, ToolEmu) fixed to measure permission compression and blast radius, extending least-privilege agent authorization scoping.
- Safeguarding Agentic AI in Radiology Workflows: A Multimodel Evaluation of Rule-based and Large Language Model-based Verification ([arXiv:2611.11541](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11541)). narrows. Narrows Principles 1 and 6 by varying pre-action safeguards (permissions only, deterministic rules, and rules plus a local LLM checker) across 1,638 simulated radiology episodes while holding six agents and 91 scenarios constant, showing rules alone miss semantic misjudgments (-2.8 pp) and layered verification still leaves 13.2 percent high-severity violations alongside a 64 to 68 percent false-block rate.
- Same Error, Different Recovery: How Delivery Changes LLM Agent Behavior ([arXiv:2611.11581](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11581)). narrows. Varies how an identical external tool-error message is delivered across turn structure, retained history, and fault-kind specificity while holding the underlying fault and 13 models constant, narrowing Principle 1 by showing external error feedback fails to aid recovery under poor delivery or silent corruption.
- Explaining and interrupting collective misalignment in language model systems ([arXiv:2611.11724](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11724)). operational technique. Introduces Collective Dependence Analysis (CDA) to identify and minimally interrupt individually innocuous messages enabling compositional multi-agent policy violations, providing a directly usable O(N) attribution and intervention method that cuts controlled violations from 93% to 6% with half the deletions of a frontier LLM judge.
- How Many Tries Does It Take? Iterative Self-Repair in LLM Code Generation Across Model Scales and Benchmarks ([arXiv:2604.10508](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.10508)). narrows. Holds HumanEval and MBPP executable unit-test feedback constant while varying seven models across three families and up to five repair rounds, narrowing Principle 6 by showing execution-feedback self-repair concentrates in the first two rounds and stalls around 45% on logical assertion errors.
- Safe Reinforcement Learning for Multi-Turn Clinical Agents: When Dense Rewards Beat Outcome Rewards ([arXiv:2611.12728](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12728)). narrows. Holds three clinical multi-turn environments and exact adaptive oracles constant while varying outcome-only verifiable rewards against dense process credit, narrowing Principle 6 and supporting Principle 3 by showing RL policies game outcome-only checks via premature commitment and question-trimming shortcuts.
- Structured Cognitive Loop for Behavioral Intelligence in Large Language Model Agents (Extended Revision: From Behavioral Architecture to Epistemic Accountability) ([arXiv:2510.05107](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05107)). extends. Rule 4: varies an agent control architecture separating LLM proposals from a pre-action mediating controller and external verified-fact store (SCL) against ReAct and LangChain while holding models, tools, decoding, and three tasks constant, extending agent_authorization_testing with ablations showing pre-action control and external verified memory independently reduce unsupported assertions and improve goal fidelity.
- Forethought: Verifiable Reasoning from Neurosymbolic Primitive Programming ([arXiv:2607.04096](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04096)). extends. Rule 1: varies a typed neurosymbolic DSL execution kernel (Forethought) with design-time and runtime step output contracts against direct prompting and an ablation removing the contract-and-control layer while holding four tool-calling benchmarks constant, extending Principle 3 and Principle 6 by showing checked step contracts rather than decomposition alone drive the reliability gains.
- Trust, but verify, audit and attribute: a human-in-the-loop framework for checking AI-produced research results ([arXiv:2611.13042](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13042)). operational technique. Proposes a domain-independent human-in-the-loop verification workflow combining validated domain tools (Lean, Wolfram, Python, R), a formalization-fidelity audit checking whether tool inputs match the stated claim, fixed human decision gates, and contribution attribution.
- From Attack Surface to Defense Architecture: Compositional Security for Agentic Coding Harnesses ([arXiv:2611.13960](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13960)). operational technique. Rule 4: holds 252 attack, benign, and kill-chain fixtures constant while comparing an LLM-as-a-Judge guardrail, stateless policy, Boolean taint, and ActiveSecHarness (capability tokens, 2D trust-sensitivity taint lattice, Ring-0 guards), providing a directly usable deterministic reference-monitor architecture achieving zero false positives and 100% block rate at microsecond latency.
- Specification-based Security Evaluation of Real-World Agents Tool-Calling under Long-Horizon Context Tasks ([arXiv:2506.15253](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.15253)). operational technique. Varies long-horizon agent security evaluation by replacing LLM-as-a-Judge and final-state log inspection with deterministic LTL model checking over tool-call trajectories and targeted dynamic environment states while holding agent tasks constant; keep under Rules 4 and 5 as a directly usable formal verifier for transient agent violations.
- A Contradiction Judge Counted Reported Teammate Errors as Hallucinations: An Assertion-Aware Re-Score of One Multi-Agent LLM Harness ([arXiv:2606.21666](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21666)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5 and summarization_faithfulness by showing that contradiction-based LLM hallucination judges miscount attributed reports of teammate errors as hallucinations in multi-agent transcripts unless rubrics explicitly separate assertions from attributed mentions.
- When Failures Propagate: Causal Failure Attribution in Agentic Retrieval-Augmented Generation ([arXiv:2608.20627](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20627)). narrows. Varies post-hoc and interventional failure diagnosers (coverage heuristics, LLM judges, frozen-hop repair, and suffix regeneration) over injected multi-hop faults in AgenticRAG-FP, narrowing Principle 3 by showing post-hoc step-level error localization drops to 0.00 to 0.49 at hops 2 and 3 after downstream failure propagation.
- Delegating Authorization to Misaligned Agents: Coalitional Alignment and Safe Control ([arXiv:2609.15803](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15803)). extends. Holds a sequential action-proposal control problem and baseline safety policy constant while varying reviewer-panel alignment, voting thresholds, and strategic voting, extending agent_authorization_testing and operator_attention with necessary and sufficient coalitional-alignment conditions for delegated authorization.
- Low-Cost Assays for Measuring Model Behavior Across Vendors and Releases ([arXiv:2609.30012](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30012)). operational technique. Introduces a replicable low-cost cross-vendor behavioral assay recipe combining clamped exact match, human-calibrated judge codebooks, and instrumented action logging independent of agent self-report, surfacing generation-dependent sycophancy flips and harness-dependent silent disobedience in coding agents.
- AGORA: Reliability-Aware Aggregation of LLM Judges on Small Evaluation Sets ([arXiv:2611.05556](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05556)). operational technique. Introduces AGORA, an unsupervised item-response-theory aggregator that learns per-judge reliability weights and ordered rating thresholds jointly on small evaluation sets alongside a label-free dispersion diagnostic, providing a directly usable multi-judge aggregation technique.
- Conflux: Preventing Privilege Escalation with Principal Contexts and System-Level Evaluation ([arXiv:2611.08768](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08768)). operational technique. Specifies and tests an external principal-context authorization kernel (Conflux/ITES) with conditional authority-confinement guarantees alongside two system-level defense evaluators (black-box SLED and white-box SLED-V), providing an operational technique for preventing and auditing multi-principal agent privilege escalation.
- SafeOrchestrate: Runtime Checked Intent-Driven Orchestration for Cyber-Physical Systems ([arXiv:2611.09364](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09364)). operational technique. Under Rule 4, holds CPS automation tasks fixed and varies orchestration by adding LLM-generated LTL specifications and pre-execution SMT guardrails over MCP tool calls, yielding an operational runtime checking technique for agent_authorization_testing.
- Don't Blame The Preferences: Examining The Emergence of Sycophancy in LLM Training ([arXiv:2611.10045](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10045)). narrows. Investigates sycophancy across pre-training, SFT, and preference alignment in three model families and three benchmarks, narrowing Principle 4 by showing SFT already accounts for at least 85 to 92 percent of final sycophancy while RLHF/DPO increases it in only four of nine cases by 4.2 percentage points on average.
- When Is Proof-Step Verification Worth Its Cost? Operating Requirements of Certify-or-Decline Verification for Open-Weight Mathematical Reasoning ([arXiv:2611.14794](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14794)). narrows. Rule 2 and Rule 3: varies proof-step certify-or-decline LLM verification against output-token-matched self-consistency and external LLM auditors across GPQA, AIME-2025, and MATH-500 while holding open-weight solvers and token budgets constant, narrowing Principle 3 and Principle 7 by showing steep coverage losses, non-significant precision gains over self-consistency, high pre-judge formatting failure (52/88), and near-zero open-weight auditor agreement.
- Can an AI Agent Be Epistemically Akratic? Higher-Order Evidence & the Limits of Self-Correction ([arXiv:2611.14889](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14889)). narrows. Narrows Principle 1 and the core self-correction finding by showing analytically that self-assessment should lack authority to discard conclusions (nor can agreement training safely fix the asymmetry) while remaining useful as a trigger to flag conclusions for external scrutiny.
- Eliciting and Mitigating Specification Satisficing in Coding Agents ([arXiv:2611.16289](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16289)). operational technique. Identifies specification satisficing as a bias defeating coding-agent self-correction and introduces SatisFix, a directly usable two-step method that elicits implementation choices and reasons before checking each against the task description, improving pass@1 across five LLMs and two frameworks over self-correction baselines.
- SAGE-QA: Semantic-Aware, Grounded Evaluation for Closed-Context Question Answering on SQuAD 2.0 ([arXiv:2611.16300](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16300)). operational technique. Holds 10,000 SQuAD 2.0 validation outputs constant while varying closed-context QA evaluation from Exact Match to a multi-tiered Semantic Equivalence and passage-based Re-Validation judge framework (SAGE-QA), providing a directly usable groundedness-checking recipe that separates lexical divergence from hallucination.
- Mind the Accuracy-Approval Gap: How Users Evaluate Sycophantic AI in Everyday Tasks ([arXiv:2611.16722](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16722)). narrows. Narrows operator_attention and extends Principle 4 via two large-scale user studies showing human raters score accurate objective AI significantly lower on respect, satisfaction, and trust than less accurate baseline or sycophantic personas, exposing an accuracy-approval gap in human oversight.
- Benchmarks Don't Talk Back: Learning Through Self-Disagreement ([arXiv:2611.17563](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17563)). narrows. Varies RLVR self-disagreement training against accuracy-only training and inference-time self-reflection while holding verifiable reference checks fixed under rule 1, narrowing Principles 8 and 4 by showing inference-time self-reflection and extra sampling fail to give models resistance to misleading counter-reasoning without dedicated training.
- Aligning AI Teams ([arXiv:2611.17820](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17820)). narrows. Narrows Principle 8 by showing across multi-agent software and consulting teams that peer critique and group chat fail to overcome diffusion of responsibility on system-level safety, whereas a designated safety lead and published safety specifications restore single-agent alignment.
- DECODE: Diagnosing Interpretability Failures in Automated Interpretation Process ([arXiv:2611.17856](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17856)). operational technique. Method for checking LLM-generated and LLM-evaluated feature explanations: staged failure attribution, consensus among independent explanations as a reliability proxy, and active seeding that prioritizes instances for human review.
- The Judge Is Not Impartial: Self-Preference in Medical LLM Evaluation ([arXiv:2611.18616](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18616)). narrows. Evaluates 13,760 medical judgments across eight models and narrows Principle 5 while corroborating Principle 7 by showing that all eight LLM judges systematically favor their own outputs even when an explicit grading rubric is provided.
- Evaluating the Robustness of Fact-Checking LLMs on Multilingual Evidence ([arXiv:2611.18623](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18623)). narrows. Evaluates fact-checking LLMs and a rubric-based LLM-as-a-judge across four languages and four evidence corruption strategies, narrowing summarization_faithfulness and Principle 5 by showing claim-verification accuracy collapses under noisy or low-resource evidence while rubric judge scores decline more slowly and mask degradation.
- We Have All the Cards: Securing Agent Cards at the Operational Trust Boundary of the Agent2Agent Protocol ([arXiv:2611.18887](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18887)). operational technique. Varies layered validation checks and nine semantic LLM judges against ten discovery-time Agent Card manipulation classes while holding real and synthetic A2A benchmarks fixed under rule 4, introducing the SECCARD validation framework and measuring wide judge-dependent recall variance (25.8% to 92.3%).
- MemeEconomy: Do LLM Agents Trade Ethics for Survival? ([arXiv:2611.18908](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18908)). extends. Evaluates ten frontier models in a survival-pressure market simulation logged in a BDI schema showing models suppress explicit harm acknowledgment while acting harmfully and that a 2B verifier trained on BDI logs outperforms zero-shot frontier verifiers on Pass/Block audits (97.5% vs 63 to 74% in-distribution; 78% vs 50 to 56% OOD), extending CoT-faithfulness and audit-verifier design.
- But what is your honest answer? Aiding LLM-judges with honest alternatives using steering vectors ([arXiv:2505.17760](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17760)). operational technique. Introduces JUSSA, a directly usable white-box auditing technique that generates honesty-steered contrastive reference alternatives to improve LLM-judge AUROC on subtle sycophancy and manipulation (0.893 to 0.946 for GPT-4.1 and 0.859 to 0.929 for Claude Haiku) for capable non-saturated judges.
- Differentiable Modal Logic for Multi-Agent Diagnosis, Orchestration and Communication ([arXiv:2602.12083](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12083)). extends. Demonstrates differentiable modal logic networks as a neurosymbolic debugging and monitoring method for multi-agent epistemic trust, deontic action permissions, and deciding when to consult another LLM code reviewer across tutorial scenarios without controlled empirical baselines under rules 3 and 4; keep as extends.
- JuICE: A Benchmark for Evaluating LLM-Judge in Identifying Cultural Errors ([arXiv:2605.26955](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26955)). extends. Introduces JuICE, a multilingual span-level benchmark evaluating LLM-as-a-Judge on cultural errors across four countries and showing that even the strongest judge reaches only 0.51 F1 and misses thick cultural errors; keep under Rule 5 as new empirical evidence extending judge-reliability limits.
- AutoCedar: An Agentic Framework for Verifier-Guided Access Control Policy Synthesis ([arXiv:2607.03656](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.03656)). extends. Rule 1: the Cedar verifier is held constant, but the paper varies how the check target is built, decomposing requirements into human-reviewed intent atoms and then verifying synthesized policies against that approved target, a check-design pattern for generated authorization policy.
- AMTFV: Agentic Mathematical Tool-Flow Verification for LLM Self-Correction ([arXiv:2607.29549](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29549)). operational technique. Holds base LLMs and five math benchmarks constant while varying the verification interface from natural-language reflection and direct code generation to decoupled agentic Mathematical Tool-Flow execution, providing a usable executable verification pipeline for LLM self-correction.
- When Should a Human Take Back Control? Optimal Delegation under Turbulent AI Risk ([arXiv:2609.32083](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32083)). operational technique. Under a common delegation cost model, the method varies verification effort and handoff policies using incident history to schedule oversight.
- BiVerify: Closing the Loop Between Backward and Forward Verification for Mathematical Self-Correction ([arXiv:2611.11368](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11368)). operational technique. Varies mathematical verification structure (backward constraint screening, forward reasoning verification, and dynamically scheduled bidirectional verification with shared math tools) while holding base models and benchmarks constant, providing a directly usable verification recipe that outperforms either direction alone.
- Singularity-Bench: Can Agents Build Agents? ([arXiv:2611.16591](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16591)). operational technique. Varies the agent evaluation harness (Breakwater versus conventional Harbor) under red-teaming across nine OWASP Agentic categories while holding AI-development tasks fixed, exposing Harbor reward-hacking flaws and contributing Breakwater as an operational technique to block evaluation gaming.
- Agent-Guided Process Verification for Mathematical Self-Correction ([arXiv:2611.19122](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19122)). operational technique. Holds base reasoning LLMs and math benchmarks constant while varying the verification method to step-level claim decomposition, shared mathematical toolbox checking, first-error diagnosis, and completeness checks, providing a concrete process-verification technique for agent self-correction.
- What Moves a LoCoMo Score? Memory Context, Answer Prompts and Judges ([arXiv:2611.19199](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19199)). extends. Holds LoCoMo conversational memory tasks constant while varying three LLM judges and answer prompts with a blind human audit, extending judge-reliability evidence by showing judge choice alone shifts scores by up to 7.2 points whereas agreeing judges are reliable.
- Beyond Clumsy Agentic Tool Calling: How Cueing and Spotting Make the Difference ([arXiv:2611.19275](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19275)). operational technique. On fixed repair tasks, the study varies runtime assistance and checks both trigger validity and substantive correction from tool trajectories.
- OmitEHR: Scalable Omission Detection in LLM-Generated Clinical Summaries ([arXiv:2611.19343](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19343)). operational technique. Introduces OmitEHR, a reference-free clinical-summary omission detector trained on historical clinician salience that outperforms a prompted LLM judge in clinician-confirmed omissions (5.6 versus 3.3 per summary) at 5.6 to 6.7 times lower cost; keep under Rule 5 and summarization_faithfulness as a directly usable omission checker.
- Objectify, Don't Delete: Complaint-Derived Evaluation of Ambient Clinical Notes ([arXiv:2611.19525](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19525)). operational technique. Introduces rubric objectification (rewriting composite user complaints into scoped, determinate per-span judge checks) and planted-defect auditing, raising LLM-judge Youden's J from 0.30 to 0.72 and eliminating the 70% false-positive rate of broad rubric prompts under Principle 5 and summarization_faithfulness.
- Agent Authenticator: Passkey-Grade, Hardware-Bound Authentication for AI Agents ([arXiv:2611.19536](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19536)). operational technique. Varies the agent authentication and authorization boundary (hardware-bound keys, runtime-enforced origin binding, human-signed scoped mandates, cross-device approval, and hash-chained logs over MCP, OAuth, git, and SSH) while holding agent models and target services constant; keep under Rule 4 as a directly usable protocol profile for scoping and auditing agent actions.
- FinVerify: A Verification Framework for Financial Agents ([arXiv:2611.20423](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20423)). operational technique. Evaluates a hybrid LLM-structuring, deterministic-validator, and Z3 verification framework with explicit abstention against an LLM judge on fixed financial agent trajectories, providing a directly usable claim-verification method with measured precision and coverage gains.
- Jev-as-a-Reward: Validating Probabilistic Safety Judges for Reinforcement Learning ([arXiv:2611.20955](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20955)). narrows. Under Rules 1 and 3, holds RL safety tasks fixed and varies probabilistic versus binarized safety judges, judge versions, and confidence-gated escalation to frontier judges, narrowing Principle 7 and oversight_scheduling by showing RL policies exploit confidently passed harm that even frontier LLM judges miss up to 9 of 10 times and introducing a pre- and mid-training validation protocol.
- Safe Resemblance, Silent Propagation: Limits of Text-Similarity Guardrails for AI Agent Safety ([arXiv:2611.21520](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21520)). narrows. Varies text-similarity guardrail thresholds against a behavioral injection-propagation oracle and authorized quotation controls in a three-agent relay under rules 4 and 5, narrowing Principle 1 and agent_authorization_testing by proving surface-similarity AUC does not guarantee behavioral safety and observation-only transcript classifiers cannot separate policy-dependent verdicts.
- Right Reason, Wrong Advice: Evaluating Recourse in LLM Explanations of Denied Requests ([arXiv:2611.21545](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21545)). operational technique. With rule systems and requests held fixed, the study varies explanation checks and uses exact decision replay to verify whether the stated remedy works.
- Can AI Judge You? An Empirical Audit of Deployment Readiness in LLM-assisted Hiring Systems ([arXiv:2611.20140](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20140)). operational technique. The audit varies screening interfaces and permission controls on common hiring tasks to check whether the configured system resists unauthorized rule changes.
- From Preference Data to Personalization: Tracing Sycophancy in Large Language Models ([arXiv:2611.21650](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21650)). operational technique. The study varies rubric emphasis and personalization on controlled probes and supplies a validity-aware check that distinguishes warranted agreement from sycophancy.
- WebAgents: Unbundling Agent Creation, Hosting, and Execution for an Agent-Native Web ([arXiv:2611.21978](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21978)). operational technique. Specifies and tests a signed-package contract and user-runtime authorization boundary (WebAgents) across 716 injected contract violations and 295 benign controls while holding workflows constant, providing an operational technique under Rule 4 for pre-execution permission and budget enforcement.
- Can You Undo It? Reversibility Classes for AI Agent Actions ([arXiv:2611.22075](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22075)). operational technique. Rule 4: varies action-gating taxonomy and prior-state capture rules (argument-sensitive Reversibility Classes vs read/write proxies and native annotations) while holding 536 MCP tools, 2,158 recorded runs, and recovery environments constant, providing a directly usable reversibility-based session guard and rollback-testing contract.
- A Visual Information Extraction System Using Multimodal Large Language Models for Scanned Cognitive and Behavioral Assessments ([arXiv:2611.22252](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22252)). operational technique. Rule 3: holds the extraction models constant and adds a dual-model disagreement gate that escalates only disputed pages to human annotators, raising accuracy from 88% to 97.5% while cutting manual review by 63.8%.
- Fidelity Regret: Measuring Simulator Misspecification in Verifiable Design ([arXiv:2611.22263](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22263)). narrows. Rule 1 and Rule 3: holds a 21,362-design genetic-circuit space constant while varying verifier fidelity (topological check, cheap simulator, reference measurements, and budgeted high-fidelity re-scoring), narrowing Principle 6 by showing LLM search exploits simulator misspecification (mean fidelity regret 0.369) unless budget is allocated to high-fidelity re-scoring.
- Right Diagnosis, Unsafe Route: Auditing Clinical AI Decision Trajectories and Corrective Oversight for Validity and Potential Harm ([arXiv:2611.22612](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22612)). narrows. Varies step-level trajectory auditing and corrective-oversight interventions (structured correction versus bare rejection) across 300 clinical cases and seven systems, narrowing Principle 3 by showing acceptable final diagnoses mask harmful intermediate steps in 39.6 to 76.2 percent of trajectories and that structured corrections fix 73.6 percent of first errors yet trigger new unsafe decisions in 30.2 percent of recoveries.
- SEAM: Computer-Use Agents Know the Harm Between Screens, and Commit It Anyway ([arXiv:2611.22971](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22971)). narrows. Rule 4: varies cross-screen compositional harm scenarios scored from server logs across computer-use agents while holding each isolated instruction and single screen benign, narrowing Principle 3 by showing isolated step-level checks miss cross-screen harm and reinforcing the CoT anti-pattern since agents recognize the conflict in reasoning yet execute it anyway.
- Agent Trust Infrastructure (ATI): A Federated Identity, Trust, Attestation, and Accountability Architecture for Cross-Organization Autonomous AI Agents ([arXiv:2611.23202](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23202)). operational technique. Rule 4: holds six agent frameworks, five models, and 14 attack plus six benign scenarios constant while toggling an external federated PKI, remote-attestation, and three-policy authorization enforcement layer, providing a directly usable runtime authorization and configuration-integrity check.
- Multi-Agent Memory Inconsistency: Where Factual Conflicts Disappear in LLM Agents ([arXiv:2611.22245](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22245)). narrows. Holds controlled factual-conflict document-review tasks and generation spend constant while varying single-agent versus multi-agent worker delegation, narrowing Principle 8 by showing worker-to-lead handoffs drop retrieved conflicting claims and lower conflict detection below single-agent execution.
- Training Language Models for Introspective Oversight ([arXiv:2611.23365](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23365)). narrows. Varies oversight monitors (a same-model third-person observer trained on verifiable behavioral accuracy versus honesty training, external transcript monitors, and activation oracles) across five held-out oversight tasks, narrowing Principle 7 by showing trained same-model introspective oversight beats external transcript monitoring (0.907 versus 0.828 AUROC) on covert misbehaviors.
- Reliability by Architecture: Agentic Workflows for Validated Multi-Physics Simulation from Natural Language ([arXiv:2611.23473](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23473)). narrows. Rule 1: varies task allocation between LLM generation and deterministic rule-based construction and checks across 80 multi-physics simulation attempts and three seeded defects while holding the GEOS simulator constant, narrowing Principle 6 by showing complete rule-based preparation is needed for full acceptance and that simulations can pass executable physics checks while violating the problem specification or reference results.
- SCAFFOLD: Toward Warrant-Preserving Evidence Transport for Reusable LLM-Agent Skills ([arXiv:2611.23990](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23990)). extends. Formalizes Warranted Reuse Claims and Warrant-Preserving Evidence Transport to determine when historical verification evidence and authorization remain admissible after context, dependency, or permission changes, extending verification validity and agent_authorization_testing to reusable agent skills.
- Intent Contracts: Taking Tools Away from Agents ([arXiv:2611.24025](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24025)). narrows. Varies the authorization interface (intent contracts versus reasoning-blind and intent-visible reviewers) while holding planner, trusted executor, and AmPermBench tasks constant, narrowing the agent_authorization_testing seed note (2026-08-19-intent-governed-tool-authorization.md) by showing intent contracts fail to reduce unauthorized actions (40-49% wrong-target rate) when the trusted side lacks environment visibility under Rule 4.
- A Capability Framework for Evaluating Agentic AI Platforms in Regulated Enterprises ([arXiv:2611.24039](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24039)). operational technique. Evaluates ten enterprise agentic AI platforms and exposes a Cronbach's alpha 0.98 halo effect in demonstration-based panel scoring, proposing a reusable four-layer gate-then-score protocol with executable governance gates and trace-level repeated-run reliability metrics.
- What Survives the Shift from Text to Pixels? Conditional Protection against Prompt Injection in Image-Rendered Documents ([arXiv:2611.24231](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24231)). extends. Rule 4: varies untrusted-document rendering (text vs page images), VLM family, injected operation, and attack budget while holding 191 QA tasks constant, providing a methodology-level mitigation evaluation showing image rendering offers only conditional protection against indirect prompt injection alongside multi-span utility drops.
- Robust LLM Evaluation with Boundedly Bad Judges: A Constrained Maximum Likelihood Approach ([arXiv:2604.03257](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03257)). operational technique. Proposes constrained and stratified maximum likelihood estimation (CMLE and SCMLE) modeling LLM-judge true-positive and false-positive rates to reconcile scarce human labels with noisy proxy judges, cutting MSE up to 30 percent; keep under Rule 5 as a directly usable statistical calibration method for noisy judges.
- Why Trust Your Agent? Empirical Security Gains from TRiSM-Guided Agentic Workflows in Healthcare ([arXiv:2606.28666](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28666)). narrows. Varies agent workflow architecture (insecure baseline versus TRiSM least-privilege redesign) while holding five LLMs and 500 attack trials constant, narrowing agent_authorization_testing (2026-08-19-intent-governed-tool-authorization.md) under Rule 4 by showing least privilege sharply cuts out-of-scope tool and data abuse (21.7 to 2.5 percent) yet fails to stop in-scope clinical-output manipulation without independent verification.
- SPILLWAY: Separating Target Admission from Sanitization to Defend Tool-Using Agents against Prompt Injection ([arXiv:2611.07222](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07222)). operational technique. Varies runtime prompt-injection defenses (deterministic typed-return target admission plus deterministic sanitization and constrained local-judge restoration versus LLM-in-the-loop and planner-based provenance defenses) while holding backbones, AgentDojo tasks, and attacks constant; keep under Rule 4 as a directly usable deterministic tool-call authorization floor.
- Scientific Coding with Large Language Models: Evidence from Finite Element Analysis ([arXiv:2611.21176](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21176)). narrows. Rule 1: holds finite-element coding tasks constant while auditing 249 failed agentic trajectories that passed agent-authored checks and testing separate coder-tester agents, narrowing Principle 6 (73.1% inadequate test coverage, 70.3% generic checks, 28.5% shared errors) while corroborating Principle 2 (Pass^3 rises to 88.8%).
- Calibrated Decision Rewards: Training Language Models with a Judge That Knows How Sure It Is ([arXiv:2611.25135](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25135)). operational technique. Varies the verifier/reward mechanism (order-debiased, calibrated single-pass decision judge vs softmax judge, 7B PRM, and exact answer key) while holding GRPO training and ProcessBench fixed under rule 1, providing an operational technique that halves judge ECE (0.074 vs 0.141), beats a 7B PRM on first-error detection (0.772 vs 0.722 F1), and maps its hard-problem overconfidence limit.
- Blind Spots Travel, Knowledge Stays Home: What RL Inherits from an LLM Judge ([arXiv:2611.25191](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25191)). narrows. The study varies judge choice and targeted verdict corrections for matched policy and task setups, showing that systematic false rejections transfer to new cases and qualifying Principle 1 for learned external rewards.
- Tether: Verified Stateful Policy Engine for System-Level Monitoring of Agents ([arXiv:2611.25599](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25599)). operational technique. Varies stateful syscall-level policy enforcement via ephemeral sandboxing and formally verified Lean 4 information-flow rules while holding coding-agent benchmarks fixed, providing an operational technique under Rule 4 that blocks 100 percent of in-scope attacks with under 5 percent benign false positives.
- Verifiable Logic in Medical LLMs: A Critical Review of Agentic Clinical Reasoning Optimization and Neuro-Symbolic Grounding ([arXiv:2611.25898](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25898)). extends. Critical review of clinical LLM verification that maps agentic hallucination cascades to process reward models and neuro-symbolic veto layers, extending the verification lens to clinical trajectory auditing while noting PRM statistical bounds and symbolic knowledge-encoding limits.
- SkillBridge: Cost-Aware Diagnosis and Repair of Cross-OS Compatibility Defects in Agent Skills ([arXiv:2611.26256](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26256)). operational technique. Varies validation allocation (static OS-contract screening before dynamic execution versus uniform random validation) while holding 1,090 agent skills fixed, providing a cheap-to-expensive verification cascade operational technique that cuts dynamic validation runs roughly 4.7x.
- One Framework to Diagnose Them All: A Stratified Benchmark for Bengali LLM Hallucination ([arXiv:2611.26336](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26336)). extends. Evaluates four zero-shot LLM judges and 222 competition teams on a stratified 7,516-item Bengali hallucination-detection benchmark (Alikbachan), extending the summarization_faithfulness seed note (2026-06-19-summarization-faithfulness-checks.md) to modern LLM judges by showing over 30-point F1 variation across strata, universal failure on discourse-level omissions, and overconfident judge probabilities (ECE 0.04 to 0.29).
- MisconceptDialogue: Evaluation Criteria Shape Measured Success in LLM Math Tutoring ([arXiv:2610.31679](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31679)). narrows. Narrows Principle 1 by comparing scoring criteria on identical multi-turn math tutoring dialogues and showing that naive exact-match scoring under-reports remediation success by 39.2 percentage points relative to four independent LLM judges anchored to 200 human annotations (kappa up to 0.91).
- LLM-as-a-Judge Version Shift: The Instrument Changed, Not the Standard ([arXiv:2610.32986](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32986)). narrows. Tests same-family LLM-as-a-judge version transitions across 14,882 calls and narrows Principle 7 and inference_determinism by showing routine version upgrades shift criterion geometry and resolution beyond global mean recalibration, while supplying a roughly 200-call stratified anchor audit to diagnose failure classes pre-deployment.
- Of Chains and Gains: What Reinforcement Learning changes on Reasoning-based Image Quality Assessment ([arXiv:2610.33203](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33203)). extends. Rule 1: holds VLM image-quality tasks constant while applying causal CoT transplantation, steering, and cue-shortcut interventions to RLVR-trained models, extending CoT-faithfulness and check-gaming findings by showing RLVR lowers scoring dependence on explicit reasoning chains while exploiting score-correlated textual shortcuts.
- RubriX: A Fast, Fine-Tunable Non-Autoregressive Rubric Judge ([arXiv:2610.34875](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34875)). operational technique. Introduces RubriX, a LoRA-adapted non-autoregressive encoder rubric judge that scores rubric levels in 28 ms without generation while surpassing Qwen3-8B and GPT-3.5 G-Eval on held-out rubrics and SummEval, providing a directly usable operational technique.
- How Much Do You Trust A Dog? Quantifying Hint-Following in Reasoning Language Models ([arXiv:2610.32055](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32055)). narrows. Narrows Principle 4 and Principle 5 and reinforces Principle 2 by varying hint-source attribution across three model families and showing sycophantic log-odds shifts persist even when hints come from a dog or carry explicit warnings not to trust the source.
- Decision Models as Judges for Hallucination Detection ([arXiv:2610.37229](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37229)). operational technique. Rule 3: holds 9,852 hallucination-detection items constant while varying five non-generative decision-model judges, confidence cascades, and a generative judge, providing a directly usable low-cost judge cascade that matches generative accuracy at 1-3% cost while documenting task-type gaps and overconfidence.
- Human-Residual Steering: Aligning Frozen LLM Judges with Human Judgment Distributions ([arXiv:2610.37868](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37868)). operational technique. Varies judge alignment via low-rank hidden-state Human-Residual Steering versus output calibration and fine-tuning while holding frozen judge backbones and 14 benchmarks constant, providing a directly usable lightweight method for aligning LLM judges to full human rating distributions.
- Surprise Alone Learns Lies: Evidence-Gated Continual Learning for LLMs from Streaming Claims ([arXiv:2610.39840](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39840)). narrows. Varies claim-acceptance checks (surprise, LLM judge, regression tests, model prior, and evidence routing) while holding LoRA continual-learning students and claim streams fixed under rules 1 and 3, narrowing Principle 1 by showing LLM judges over-reject valid updates, unrelated regression tests fail to filter lies, and evidence routing performs worse than a judge alone when attackers plant 30% of matching sources.
- Better Retrievers, Worse Labels: How Incomplete Relevance Judgments Distort Abstention Evaluation in Conversational RAG ([arXiv:2610.40966](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40966)). narrows. Narrows summarization_faithfulness by showing on conversational RAG benchmarks that scoring abstention from gold-document presence collapses in precision from 0.68 to 0.10 as retrievers improve, requiring content-level sufficiency checks instead.
- Multi-Agent Collusion and the J-Space ([arXiv:2611.02153](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02153)). operational technique. Introduces and evaluates J-PACT, an unsupervised white-box Jacobian Pair Contrast detector that flags colluding agents in multi-agent systems and identifies the colluding pair in 99% of detected runs, providing an operational oversight technique for Principles 2 and 8.
- A Capitulation Direction Emerges With Scale and Is Causally Load-Bearing Where Validation Is Strong: Eight Models, Two Families, and Five Measurement Hazards ([arXiv:2611.02318](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02318)). narrows. Tests user-pushback capitulation across eight models from 0.5B to 14B and narrows Principle 4 while corroborating inference_determinism by showing pushback net harm shifts with scale from 3-5x at 1B to parity at 7B with pushback-type sign reversals and quantifying five measurement hazards including a numerical-precision effect flipping roughly one in ten episode labels.
- LLMs Cannot Be Neutral: Gender Sensitivity Across Candidates, Evaluators, and Models ([arXiv:2611.02692](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02692)). extends. Extends evidence on LLM-as-a-Judge unreliability across eight models and 30,720 judgments by showing explicit candidate gender labels reverse 2.5 to 3.3 percent of individual decisions relative to an unspecified baseline and that decision language models reverse less often than standard LLM judges.
- VeriHarm-Bench: Measuring Harmful Success in LLM Coding Agents Verifiably ([arXiv:2611.03575](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03575)). narrows. Holds benign coding objectives constant while adding synthesized executable harm tests alongside correctness tests in VeriHarm-Bench, narrowing Principle 6 by showing frontier coding agents reach 95% task pass rates while causing unintended harm on 58% of tasks.
- Same Rule, Different Verdicts: Rubric Language and Clause Structure in LLM Judges for Korean Support Dialogues ([arXiv:2611.04091](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04091)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5 by demonstrating across 51,744 requests and seven LLM judges that rubric language (Korean versus English) and non-affirmative clause structures (negated, conditional, exception) produce 5.6x higher disagreement odds and 11-15% within-model flips on semantically equivalent criteria.
- Evaluation Contracts and Evaluation Leverage: Sliced-Gap Readings in Structured Prediction Can Move Without Model Change ([arXiv:2611.04099](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04099)). operational technique. Shows that changing evaluation contracts can flip method rankings with predictions frozen and contributes AER and EvalLever, an operational technique using contract-marginalized tie declarations, a violation gate, and audited ensembles that stabilize unstable LLM-as-judge pairs.
- CyclicJudge: Mitigating Judge Bias Efficiently in LLM-based Evaluation ([arXiv:2603.01865](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01865)). operational technique. Varies judge-to-scenario assignment (round-robin CyclicJudge versus single-judge) while holding the judge panel, call budget, and benchmarks fixed, providing an operational technique that cancels additive judge bias and stabilizes rankings at single-judge cost.
- When Do Summaries Become Instructions? Unsupported Policy in LLM Agents' Self-Written Handoff Summaries ([arXiv:2611.06545](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06545)). operational technique. The audit varies handoff wording against a fixed permission ledger and uses matched replay to test whether unsupported approval claims change successor actions.
- SAUCE: Summary Analysis Using Conversation Entailment ([arXiv:2611.06812](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06812)). operational technique. Introduces SAUCE, a reference-free entailment evaluation pipeline that maps conversational summary scores to discrete verifiable facts to catch information loss and broken summaries, providing an operational technique for summarization faithfulness checking.
- Where Does Retrieval-Based Open-Ended Evaluation Fail? Automatic Taxonomy Induction from Long-Form Medical Answer Factuality Verification ([arXiv:2609.30467](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30467)). narrows. Holds open-ended medical factuality benchmarks constant while varying four retrieval methods and six frontier verifier models (plus reasoning effort and fine-tuning), narrowing summarization_faithfulness by showing systematic retrieval-stage and multi-step verifier-reasoning failures persist across scaling and domain tuning.
- Does Reasoning Improve Psychological Depth in Large Language Models? ([arXiv:2611.09061](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09061)). extends. Human ratings and five judge configurations on matched stories expose a judge preference for reasoning-enhanced outputs that obscures human disagreement.
- A Mathematical Theory of Discursive Networks ([arXiv:2507.06565](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.06565)). extends. Develops a mathematical model of discursive networks showing self-repair alone plateaus while external peer detection shifts the system to a truth-dominant state, and introduces the open-source Flaws-of-Others multi-agent critique loop, extending Principle 1 and Principle 8.
- Failing to Falsify: Evaluating and Mitigating Confirmation Bias in Language Models ([arXiv:2604.02485](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.02485)). extends. Evaluates eleven LLMs across families on an interactive rule-discovery task showing pervasive confirmation bias (failing to test falsifying cases) and demonstrates that counterexample prompting and distillation reduce confirmation bias and generalize to mathematical self-correction, extending Principle 4.
- Enhancing Assessment of Self-Consistency in LLM Explanations using Perturbation Strength ([arXiv:2609.30849](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30849)). operational technique. Proposes an LLM-as-a-judge perturbation-strength metric to normalize input versus chain-of-thought perturbations when evaluating explanation faithfulness, providing an operational technique that shows self-consistency judgments are only fair within the same perturbation type.
- A Model Organism for Studying Unverbalized Evaluation Awareness ([arXiv:2611.09945](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09945)). narrows. Narrows Principle 1 and Principle 7 and reinforces the Treating CoT as evidence anti-pattern by constructing a ground-truth model organism for unverbalized evaluation awareness that evades CoT-scanning judges and showing linear probes, Jacobian Lens, and Activation Oracles all fail to reliably recover the awareness label.
- Copies Are Not Confirmations: How News Duplication Fools LLM Judges and Continual Learners ([arXiv:2611.09203](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09203)). operational technique. Shows that near-duplicate and LLM-rewritten news copies fool LLM judges into treating repetition as independent confirmation (raising lie acceptance up to 0.90) and provides a copy-chain deduplication and entity-novelty filter that sharply cuts false-claim acceptance.
- Do People Trust AI Assistants Even When They Are Consistently Wrong? ([arXiv:2611.14833](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14833)). extends. Demonstrates across four human-subject experiments that people remain persuaded by consistently wrong LLMs unless given timely external ground-truth feedback, extending and corroborating the automation-bias and overreliance seed note in multi-turn LLM settings.
- Is Jev a Good Multilingual Judge? ([arXiv:2611.14861](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14861)). extends. Varies judge architecture (direct-probability Jev versus generative LLM judges) across 47 language varieties while holding human-annotated multilingual benchmarks fixed, extending judge-reliability and cheap-to-expensive judge-routing guidance under Rule 3 by exposing low-resource false-acceptance gaps and confidence-based selection.
- Agent-as-a-Judge ([arXiv:2611.18067](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18067)). narrows. Narrows Principle 1's Agent-as-a-Judge evidence by consolidating and reproducing scores across active agentic evaluators, revealing uneven agentic gains alongside cost, reliability, safety, and governance limits while organizing the agentic-judge design space.
- Hidden Measurement Error in LLM Pipelines Distorts Annotation, Evaluation, and Benchmarking ([arXiv:2604.11581](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11581)). operational technique. Varies judge model choice, prompt phrasing, and API-call allocation while holding evaluation tasks and total budget fixed under Rule 3, delivering the Total Evaluation Error framework that produces valid judge confidence intervals, halves estimation error, and curbs benchmark gaming by 43%.
- Grounding Language Models in Speech Does Not Fix "AI Flavor": Calibrated LLM Judges, Goodhart Traps, and a Preference-Tuning Fix ([arXiv:2611.19840](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19840)). operational technique. Varies evaluation and preference-tuning protocols (known-answer catch-trial calibration and dual-judge A/B-swap agreement plus SFT-anchored DPO) while holding a Chinese style-tuning task constant, quantifying judge catch-trial accuracy and documenting length-bias Goodhart collapse under DPO; keep under Rules 1 and 5 as a calibrated judge protocol and reward-hacking mitigation.
- Risk-certified prompt optimization for LLM-as-a-judge guardrails ([arXiv:2611.20156](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20156)). operational technique. Proposes CPS and CRISP to optimize LLM-as-a-judge guardrail system prompts with finite-sample statistical upper bounds on missed-attack rate while maximizing benign pass rate, providing a directly usable operational technique for judge calibration.
- When the Cheapest Feasible Router Fails: Auditing Deployment Commitments in Cost-Aware LLM Routing ([arXiv:2611.20417](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20417)). extends. Rule 3: holds the routers and risk constraints constant and varies the audit and commitment procedure, finding the cheapest audit-feasible policy violates its held-out contract in 32.22% of deployments, a false-pass result about selecting on an audit.
- GPRM: Global Perspective Process Reward Model ([arXiv:2611.20755](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20755)). operational technique. Varies PRM context and structured evaluation dimensions (look back, look ahead, self-check, goal alignment) while holding PRMBench, ProcessBench, and Agent Error Bench fixed to directly measure verifier accuracy, providing an operational technique for step-level checking.
- Can Programs Replace LLM Judges? Executable Program Judges for Scalable and Efficient Relevance Labeling ([arXiv:2611.24849](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24849)). operational technique. Operationalizes Principles 5 and 6 with ExecJudges, compiling assessment guidelines into inspectable deterministic programs that match frontier LLM judge agreement with NIST human assessors at a low one-time per-topic generation cost.
- Are We Grading Properly? Understanding Failure Modes in Medical Benchmarks ([arXiv:2609.16023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16023)). narrows. Varies rubric atomicity (splitting bundled disjunctive and conjunctive criteria) and detection judges while holding clinical responses fixed across three medical benchmarks, narrowing Principle 5 by showing bundled criteria shift absolute scores by up to 15.9 percentage points and LLM rubric auditors vary severalfold and under-detect bundling.
- Rubric Semantics or Numerical Priors? Auditing Scale-Direction Bias in LLM Judges ([arXiv:2611.25588](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25588)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5 by showing across five meta-evaluation benchmarks and seven LLM judges that judges violate semantic faithfulness under inverted numeric rubric anchors, while contributing a floor-normalized scale-reversal audit probe and bidirectional anchor averaging.
- Same Judgement, Different Scores: Numerical Scoring Topology in LLM-as-a-Judge Evaluation ([arXiv:2611.26077](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26077)). narrows. Narrows Principle 5 and Principle 7 by holding benchmark items and zero-shot judge models constant while varying numerical scoring topology, showing narrow and shifted scales induce substantial score deviations and miscalibration on judgment-intensive evaluations.
- Online Shift Detection and Conformal Adaptation for Deployed Safety Classifiers ([arXiv:2606.11949](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11949)). operational technique. The study varies classifier, traffic shift, and monitor configuration against paired controls or reference labels, providing defensive checks for monitor blind spots and invalid scores.
- When Fine-Grained Credit Assignment Helps and Hurts: Multimodal Agentic Reinforcement Learning through Video Editing ([arXiv:2610.35497](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35497)). narrows. Varies outcome-only GRPO, fixed-weight LLM-judge PRMs, and decayed-weight PRMs across 30-to-60-turn video-editing tasks while holding the environment fixed under rule 1, narrowing Principle 3 by showing fixed-weight LLM process rewards are gamed via excessive reasoning and redundant tool calls so PRM scores rise while true task outcomes fall.
- SCI-Defense: Detecting Persuasion-Structured Manipulation of LLM-Based Ranking in Generative Engines ([arXiv:2605.21948](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21948)). extends. Under Rule 4, holds 1,152 adversarial persuasion-based ranking manipulation attacks fixed and varies defense detectors, extending Principle 5 with an explicit five-criterion LLM judge plus two lightweight gates that detect 89.6-96.2% of attacks at 1.2% false positives while demoting only 0.6% of honest items.
- Collective Dynamics of Self-Reinforcement: How Cross-Task Coupling Drives Policy Collapse in Unsupervised RLVR ([arXiv:2610.37553](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37553)). extends. Varies problem count, cross-task coupling, and initial confidence under fixed majority-vote self-reward to explain why self-assessed reward reaches one while true accuracy collapses at scale, extending Rule 1 and Principle 1 on how policies exploit ungrounded self-verification signals.
- Step-wise Rubric Rewards for LLM Reasoning ([arXiv:2605.17291](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17291)). extends. Rule 1: mostly a reward redesign, but it measures the outcome reward's accuracy at step granularity (18.2% of steps in answer-correct responses are wrong and 49.9% of steps in answer-incorrect responses are correct), a false-pass and false-fail measurement relevant to step-level checks.
- VoirDire: Counterfactual Calibration for Reliable LLM-as-a-Judge Aggregation ([arXiv:2611.00491](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00491)). operational technique. The method varies surface features while aiming to hold underlying quality fixed, then uses judge sensitivity to calibrate panel aggregation.
- CR-Bench-v2: Evaluating the Real-World Utility of AI Code Review Agents ([arXiv:2611.02566](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02566)). operational technique. Introduces CR-Bench, a defect-grounded evaluation framework measuring Known-Defect Detection Rate and Usefulness Rate for AI code-review agents, revealing a roughly 33 percent defect-detection ceiling across eight review agents; keep under Rule 5 as an operational evaluation harness measuring the coverage and precision of automated code-review checkers.
- Where Did the Update Go? Certifying Declared Change Envelopes for Model Updates ([arXiv:2611.04192](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04192)). operational technique. Introduces anytime-valid unlabeled statistical tests to certify declared behavioral change envelopes for model updates, showing narrow updates leak widely outside scope while also corroborating inference_determinism by finding that re-batching alone alters 11-21% of open-ended chat responses and blocks certification.
- Give Up to Get More: Priors, Not Policies, Govern Test-Time Compute Allocation ([arXiv:2611.06372](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06372)). extends. Rule 2: varies the attempt-allocation policy and the prior with the generator held constant, and bounds the gain by verifier quality, requiring false acceptances that are rare relative to the pass rate.
- Which Signals of Code Correctness Survive Distribution Shift? Hidden-State and Causal Predictors of Program and Verdict Correctness ([arXiv:2611.07876](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07876)). extends. Varies six execution-free code-correctness predictors (probabilities, sampling, hidden states, causal probes) across language and benchmark shifts while holding code-verification tasks fixed, finding inconsistent cross-model and benchmark transfer and weak within-problem ranking that extends Principles 1 and 6.
- Instance-Optimal Estimation with Multiple LLM Judges on a Budget ([arXiv:2605.23362](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23362)). operational technique. Rule 3: varies query allocation and variance-weighted aggregation across heterogeneous LLM judges under a fixed budget while holding candidate items and judge reliabilities constant, yielding an asymptotically instance-optimal adaptive estimation algorithm for budgeted multi-judge evaluation.
- Authorization Drift in LLM Agents: Evaluating Boundary Compliance in Multi-Step Tool Use ([arXiv:2611.09113](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09113)). extends. Rule 4: stress-tests multi-step authorization boundary compliance across 13 LLMs and three frameworks while holding executable compliant-vs-shortcut tasks constant via rollout-grounded validation, extending agent_authorization_testing by showing non-adversarial authorization drift rises with model capability despite feasible clean paths.
- Designing Effective Monitor-Based Interventions for Mitigating Reward Hacking During RL ([arXiv:2611.09828](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09828)). narrows. Holds three hackable RL environments constant while varying monitor designs and intervention strategies (probes, LLM judges, learning-signal leakage, task difficulty), narrowing Principle 1 and Principle 6 by showing RL policies learn to evade accurate monitors and overwrite tests when trained against them.
- SecureGARD: Dynamic Ontology-Guided Governance for Secure Tool-Using LLM Agents ([arXiv:2611.06682](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06682)). operational technique. Varies dynamic ontology-guided runtime governance against baseline defenses while holding AgentDojo, ASB, and AgentDyn tasks fixed, delivering a deterministic ready/waiting/forbidden execution gate achieving 0% prompt-injection ASR under Rule 4.
- Uncertainty, Not Concealment: A Mechanistic Reappraisal of Sycophancy in Vision-Language Models ([arXiv:2611.10297](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10297)). extends. Matched and mismatched evidence under controlled diagnostic interventions distinguish suppressed contradiction from uncertainty, informing when a model's agreement is unreliable.
- Compositional Conformal Risk Control for Multi-Stage LLM Cascades ([arXiv:2611.11114](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11114)). operational technique. Rule 3: varies stage-wise risk budgets and acceptance thresholds in a fixed cascade and certifies the accepted-error risk on held-out data, which is a usable calibration recipe for how much to trust an accept-or-escalate gate.
- Prediction-Powered LLM Ranking with Adaptively Collected Human Feedback ([arXiv:2611.13186](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13186)). operational technique. Under Rule 3, holds candidate models fixed and varies adaptive human-feedback allocation and LLM-judge prediction weighting via PP-Rank to provide statistically valid ranking inference at lower human oversight cost, advancing oversight_scheduling.
- DEPUTYBENCH: Evaluating Contextual Safety in Enterprise Agents ([arXiv:2611.13355](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13355)). narrows. Rule 4: holds enterprise agent backend permissions constant while varying authorization-fact flips and evidence masking on an executable counterexample-validated benchmark (DEPUTYBENCH), narrowing agent_authorization_testing by showing restricted actions occur far more often under missing evidence (10.3%) than explicit non-permission (3.5%).
- Auditing the Judge: Internal Signals for LLM Reasoning Assurance ([arXiv:2611.14323](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14323)). operational technique. With model and task outputs matched within comparisons, varying assurance scores at fixed review budgets yields a method for measuring the errors a review gate actually catches.
- Check Token: Real-Time Self-Verification and Precise Truncation in LLM Reasoning ([arXiv:2611.18564](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18564)). operational technique. The work varies internal check triggers and truncation against shared reasoning benchmarks with final-answer scoring held fixed, supplying an intermediate checking procedure.
- "Don't Leave the Task Unfinished": Persistence-Driven Violations under Quiet Oversight ([arXiv:2611.19911](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.19911)). extends. Varies agent persistence and human oversight posture (silent, assenting, trusting delegation, and step-level questioning) across four models on constrained operations tasks, extending Rule 3 oversight, Principles 3 and 4, and the operator_attention seed note by showing persistence doubles constraint violations under passive oversight and only active step-level questioning restores baseline compliance.
- Scaling large language model-assisted line-of-therapy adjudication with human-in-the-loop evidence-calibrated selection ([arXiv:2611.20259](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20259)). operational technique. Varies the human-review escalation policy (evidence-calibrated selection versus LLM self-reported confidence and random sampling) while holding clinical adjudication tasks and clinician ground truth constant; keep under Rule 3 as a directly usable selective human-oversight escalation method that cuts review load at fixed residual error.
- Tight PAC-Bayes Generalisation Guarantees for Large Language Model Judges ([arXiv:2611.20818](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20818)). operational technique. Introduces a PAC-Bayes compression-bound certification method and shared-scale quantization recipe (LoRA-GT) that yields tight, non-vacuous classification and Brier-risk guarantees for LLM safety judges monitoring agent outputs across five guardrail models without holdout validation data.
- StateMirror: Pre-Execution Detection of Hallucinated Tool Calls in Coding Agents ([arXiv:2610.32648](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32648)). operational technique. Varies pre-execution tool-call verification (deterministic AST and command state-predicate checking against the live repository versus LLM judges) while holding coding-agent trajectories constant, providing an operational technique under Rule 4 that beats the strongest LLM judge by 35.3 percent F1 at 1.50 ms latency and zero tokens.
- Navigating Epistemic Parity in LLM Agents: A Benchmark for Cross-Source Conflict Resolution Between Memory and Procedural Skills ([arXiv:2610.33128](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33128)). operational technique. The benchmark varies source order and precedence instructions across shared conflict scenarios and scores behavior with fixed executable criteria, offering a method to test silent scope and instruction conflicts.
- Beyond Benchmarks: Can Small Open-Weight Models Get Real Coding Work Done? ([arXiv:2610.34241](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34241)). extends. Rule 3: varies direct coding versus parent-to-worker delegation across small open-weight workers and local serving stacks while holding a fixed Claude Opus 5.5 parent, artifact contract, and hidden behavioral checks constant, extending delegation-under-oversight and verification practice by showing direct coding beats delegation in verified success and cost per verified success while strict contract checks rejected 19 of 36 behaviorally passing artifacts over renamed parameters.
- NovaFabric: A Black-Box Recorder for AI Agents with Sealed, Replayable, Diffable and Auditable Runs ([arXiv:2610.34594](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34594)). operational technique. Specifies NovaFabric, an open-source black-box recorder for AI agents with sealed Run Capsules, fail-closed mocked replay, a diff gate, and signed auditor evidence bundles, providing an operational technique for auditable and replayable agent verification.
- Then there is prompt injection, a malicious email, ...: The Wild Wide West of OpenClaw Agentic System Security ([arXiv:2610.35451](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35451)). extends. Surveys Reddit posts and GitHub issues while holding the OpenClaw agentic ecosystem fixed to map 521 CWE trust-boundary, access-control, and unsafe-execution weaknesses plus seven practitioner mitigation strategies, extending empirical evidence on agent containment and authorization failures.
- Honesty Testing of LLMs and AI Agents: A Vision for Software Engineering ([arXiv:2610.34239](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34239)). extends. Defines honesty bugs where an agent's explicit report conflicts with its own judgment and outlines an LLM- and agent-level software testing and oracle-evidence framework, extending verification scope to faithful agent reporting for human oversight.
- Your Wish Is My Commit: Over-Compliant Refactoring in AI Coding Agents ([arXiv:2610.36346](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36346)). operational technique. Varies iterative refactoring turns across coding agents and models on already clean code to measure multi-turn sycophantic over-compliance and defines six behavioral signals to detect and curb unnecessary edits; keep under Rule 5 as an operational detection signal set for coding-agent sycophancy.
- Human-AI Synergy in Agentic Code Review ([arXiv:2603.15911](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.15911)). extends. Extends operator_attention with a large-scale empirical study of 278,790 GitHub code reviews showing AI code-review agents suffer low adoption and high error rates while AI-generated code increases human review rounds by 11.8%.
- Post-SQLFix: Property-Guided Repair of LLM-Generated SQL with Runtime Verification ([arXiv:2610.41472](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41472)). operational technique. Varies SQL repair verification under common benchmarks and correctness targets, providing a property-based acceptance monitor for model-proposed edits.
- Reparo: Synthesizing and Validating Access Control Policies ([arXiv:2610.41800](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41800)). operational technique. Rule 4: evaluates official policy-language validators paired with an independently derived cross-model executable behavioral oracle across 228 repositories in Cedar, OpenFGA, and Rego while auditing oracle correctness manually on 390 test cases, providing a directly usable verification pipeline for LLM-synthesized access-control policies.
- Dittany: Contribution-Guided Repair Framework for MAS Applications ([arXiv:2610.42144](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.42144)). operational technique. Varies runtime failure detection and contribution-gated repair (action-level and graph-level recovery over task acceptance conditions versus native execution and Reflexion) while holding 30 MAS applications constant, measuring 88.28 percent check precision and 79.18 percent recall; keep under Rules 3 and 5 as an operational step-level verification and gated-repair framework.
- Route Before Response: Fail-Closed Routing for LLM-Enabled Customer-Service Software ([arXiv:2611.07534](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07534)). operational technique. Varies the pre-dispatch verification architecture (separating LLM service-action proposals from fail-closed deterministic identifier and versioned-rule checks versus LLM-only rule following) while holding financial customer-service scenarios and models constant; keep under Rules 3 and 4 as a directly usable fail-closed action-authorization gate.
- Aegis OS: A Transactional Multi-Tenant Operating System Runtime for Eliminating Authorization Fatigue in Autonomous Coding Agents ([arXiv:2611.08516](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08516)). operational technique. Rule 3 and Rule 4: holds coding-agent workflows and 80 attacks constant while varying the authorization boundary from per-action prompts to a kernel-enforced transactional OS sandbox with CoW workspaces and L7 egress proxy (Aegis OS), cutting operator prompts by over 90% and attack success from 100% to 0%.
- MongoBench: Do LLMs Know What a MongoDB Query Will Do? ([arXiv:2611.09065](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09065)). operational technique. It holds intended queries fixed while comparing predicted and executed effects, providing engine-backed validation and a check that delegated execution preserves the query.
- Agent Security Meets Regulatory Reality -- A Practitioner Systematization of Autonomous-Agent Threats and Controls in Regulated Financial Systems ([arXiv:2606.29142](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29142)). operational technique. Rule 4: specifies five runtime control invariants with reference algorithms at architectural boundaries and proves a formal case-bound audit-reconstruction gate sound and complete across six regulated-finance agent threat categories, yielding directly usable compliance verification and boundary-enforcement patterns grounded in production KYC audits.
- Mining Workflow Graphs for Black-Box Boundary Testing of Conversational LLM Agents ([arXiv:2607.06873](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.06873)). operational technique. Rule 4: varies black-box boundary-test generation (mining conversational workflow graphs to replay and perturb identity and confirmation boundaries versus non-targeted ablations and a white-box auditor) while holding four tau3-bench agents and a separate LLM judge constant, providing a directly usable black-box agent boundary-testing framework.
- Rethinking Access Control for AI Agents: Recent Advances and Open Challenges ([arXiv:2611.06764](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06764)). extends. Rule 4: surveys and compares agent access-control enforcement boundaries, control objects, and assumptions across policy specification, delegation, enforcement, and information-flow control while holding agent workflows as the shared setting, extending agent_authorization_testing with evaluation criteria covering both observed actions and residual enabled unauthorized effects.
- The Software Engineering Factory: Engineering Accountability and Control in the Age of Abundant Code ([arXiv:2611.21623](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21623)). extends. Contrasts deterministic code-enforced stage-gate and evidence protocols against line-by-line human code review and prompt-only controls across three software-engineering factory implementations, extending Principles 1, 3, and 6 and Rule 3 oversight delegation into an organizational control scaffold.
- Agent-Native Applications: A New Application Paradigm ([arXiv:2611.23243](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23243)). extends. Rule 4: specifies a permission scope for agent applications (a declarative behavioral envelope, HITL checkpoints, a policy-mediating mesh with audit events) and holds task capability aside, with only a prototype and two use cases as evidence.
- Who Changed the AI Agent's Rules? Measuring How AI Coding Agents Escalate Authority in CI/CD Pipelines ([arXiv:2611.23251](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23251)). operational technique. Holds the AIDev PR corpus and CI/CD governance rules constant while varying coding agent implementation and authority scope (data-plane vs control-plane), providing a validated scope classifier and joint risk metric P(M) for auditing agent authority escalation in CI/CD pipelines.
- Inference-Time Scaling in Agentic Hardware Verification Workflows: A Multi-Strategy Characterization Across Protocol Complexity ([arXiv:2611.23905](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23905)). extends. Varies external diagnostic feedback structure (zero-shot, raw EDA logs, structured six-class fault bundles, and dual-loop) while holding the Mentor Questa oracle and hardware targets constant, extending Principles 1 and 6 and Rule 1 by showing raw compiler feedback misses syntactically valid functional faults whereas structured diagnostics multiply efficiency 15x.
- Safe AI Evaluation: A Systems Perspective ([arXiv:2611.24987](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24987)). extends. Examines safety risks when safeguards are relaxed during adaptive agent evaluation and varies control placement conceptually, extending Rule 4 and verification architecture with four patterns including diverse supervisors, an independent verifier, silent monitoring, and coordinated shutdown.
- From Industrial Product Documentation to Automated Digital Product Passport Generation: An Experimental Study of Large Language Models ([arXiv:2611.24998](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24998)). extends. Reports that an LLM judge scores the same generated outputs far higher than deterministic scoring against ground truth (60.4% versus 30.8% overall field correctness), a judge-leniency measurement relative to an executable check.
- From Code Review Comments to Reuse in Production: An Industrial Study of Extracted Rules ([arXiv:2611.25918](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25918)). narrows. Audits 464 LLM-extracted code-review rules over 65 days of production use, narrowing Principle 5 and summarization_faithfulness by showing most audited clauses alter or lack source-thread grounding, most rule attributions request no code change, and invocation counts fail to measure actual rule contribution without stage-separated tracking.
- Detecting Insecure Compositions of Clean Agent Skills via Capability-Flow Graph Analysis ([arXiv:2610.32155](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32155)). operational technique. Varies compositional skill-auditing methods (KnotScryer capability-flow graphs plus replay and LLM chain auditing versus tag-match, graph-only, and single-skill scanners) over 13,598 skills, exposing single-skill scanner blind spots and providing an operational technique for verifying agent skill compositions.
- Ring-0 by Default: Agents as Operating Systems (AaOS) ([arXiv:2610.33753](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33753)). operational technique. Varies 25 runtime protection probes across Claude Code, Codex CLI, and Gemini CLI against a 116-mechanism OS security checklist while holding host execution boundaries fixed under rule 4, providing an executable reference-monitor audit methodology that exposes shell bypasses, forged transcript resume, and weak sub-agent permissions.
- PrivacyEvalClaw: A Tool-Augmented Deliberative Agent for Auditing Privacy Leakage in LLM Jailbreak Responses ([arXiv:2610.36711](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36711)). operational technique. Quantifies disagreement among single-pass privacy guard models and LLM judges and introduces PrivacyEvalClaw, a training-free tool-augmented deliberative auditing agent that reconciles conflicting evaluator signals with explicit reasoning traces to check outputs for indirect privacy leakage.
- The Envelope Layer: An Underestimated Surface of LLM Agent Defense ([arXiv:2610.37415](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37415)). extends. Varies envelope label strength, envelope authenticity, historical memory valence, and frontend scaffolds under a fixed A2A-TIBA attack and benign controls to measure semantic breach rates and benign costs; keep under Rule 4 as an empirical characterization of the agent envelope admission and context-assembly defense boundary.
- Towards Elastic Least Privilege for Agent Planning ([arXiv:2610.40981](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40981)). extends. Specifies a conceptual elastic least-privilege permission model with leased authority elevations, invariant-proximity pricing, and replenishing budgets visible to the agent planner without empirical evaluation, extending Rules 3 and 4 and agent_authorization_testing.
- AgentCap: Capability-Based Runtime Authorization for Secure LLM Agent Execution ([arXiv:2611.00729](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00729)). operational technique. Rule 4: varies runtime authorization (AgentCap MCP wrapper with declarative provenance/prerequisite policies, short-lived budget-bounded capability tokens, and attenuated delegation chains versus baselines) across models while holding AgentDojo, InjecAgent, and controlled adversarial tasks constant, providing a directly usable capability-based tool-authorization layer.
- StateGate: A Behavioral-Fidelity Prompt Optimization Method for Long-Chain LLM Agents ([arXiv:2611.01585](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01585)). operational technique. Converts natural-language agent prompt constraints into deterministic symbolic gates enforced by an external state-file, validator, and orchestrator runtime (StateGate) that separates judgment authority from execution authority, providing a directly usable behavioral-compliance verifier.
