- IAMRAGBENCH: Benchmarking Authorization Expansions in LLM-Generated AWS IAM Policies under RAG Poisoning ([arXiv:2611.03431](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03431)). narrows. Rule 4: stress-tests LLM-generated AWS IAM policies under RAG poisoning with deterministic authorization-envelope scoring while holding 60 policy tasks constant, narrowing Principle 6 and agent_authorization_testing by showing AWS CheckNoNewAccess falsely clears 4 of 168 exact unauthorized-access attacks whereas a full pre-deployment envelope rule plus human escalation catches all 171 exact deltas.
- Permitted but Unauthorized: Affordance-Induced Delegated-Scope Violations in Multi-Agent LLM Systems ([arXiv:2611.04086](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04086)). narrows. Rule 4: holds multi-agent permissions constant while varying action visibility, competitive incentives, and enforcement mechanisms (reference monitor vs pre-execution judge), narrowing agent_authorization_testing by showing action representation drives crossings and that 100%-effective scope enforcement still fails to prevent harm via authorized actions on an agent's own resources.
- Jev as an LLM Safety Guardrail: An Empirical Study of Input Filtering, Selective Output Review, and Over-Refusal ([arXiv:2611.08845](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08845)). extends. Rule 3: varies safety-guardrail gating architecture and thresholds (unfiltered vs single input gate vs tiered input filtering with selective output review) while holding the Qwen2.5-1.5B generator and prompt benchmark constant, empirically characterizing false-pass vs over-refusal trade-offs and showing modest matched-budget cascade gains that do not consistently hold out of sample.
- The Devil Wears Markdown: Detecting Malicious Intent Hidden in AI Agent Skill Instructions through Hybrid Static, Dynamic, and Agentic Analysis ([arXiv:2611.10830](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10830)). operational technique. Rule 4: holds a 140-skill malicious-vs-benign test set constant while varying static, safety-constrained dynamic sandbox, and LLM-reasoning evidence layers, yielding a directly usable hybrid verification pipeline that cuts false positives from 40 to 5 at 0.89 F1.
- Agents of Chaos: A Systematization of Knowledge (SoK) on Malicious Attacks on Agentic AI Systems ([arXiv:2611.10843](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10843)). extends. Surveys 98 agentic-security papers across attack classes and safeguards rather than contributing new exploits, extending the Rule 4 and safeguard-evaluation lens by showing only 278 of 660 attack-safeguard relationships are directly assessed and strong coverage is uncommon.
- Tracing MCP Security Vulnerabilities: Constructing and Analyzing an MCP Security Vulnerability Dataset ([arXiv:2611.11104](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11104)). extends. Under Rule 4, audits 119 CVE disclosures to build a validated dataset of 24 MCP security vulnerabilities and maps defenses rather than exploits, extending agent_authorization_testing with empirical prevalence of inadequate access control, injection, and path traversal in agent tool protocols.
- Text2NGAC: a multi-LLM pipeline for NGAC policy extraction with mutation-based verification ([arXiv:2611.12572](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12572)). operational technique. Under Rule 4, holds natural-language access-control corpora fixed and varies verification via a five-strategy NGAC graph verifier tested with 918 controlled mutations (catching 99.7% of mutants and all structural fail-open faults) plus uncertainty-triggered ensemble escalation, delivering an operational technique for agent_authorization_testing.
- VRMaaS: Verifiable Reward-Model-as-a-Service for Trustworthy LLM Alignment ([arXiv:2611.14984](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14984)). operational technique. Varies reward-model service attestation (VRMaaS proof-carrying artifacts binding request, weights, and numerical configuration versus generic inference proofs) while holding 1B to 8B reward models fixed, rejecting six service-field substitution attacks as an operational technique for auditing external reward services.
- Intent-Aware Protection for AI Agents ([arXiv:2611.16122](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16122)). operational technique. Under Rule 4, holds agent tasks and environment-state ground truth fixed and varies pre-execution, post-execution, and input-alignment checks grounded in an Intent Profile, providing an operational technique for agent_authorization_testing that lifts out-of-scope F1 from 8% to 76% and reaches 0.949 AUROC on over 10,000 AgentDojo trajectories.
- GapLeak: When Privacy-Preserving LLM-based Web Agents Become a Privacy Risk ([arXiv:2611.22223](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22223)). narrows. Rule 4: varies benign web-form information gaps, instruction-level least-privilege defenses, and submission-time egress verification across three web-agent frameworks and six LLMs while holding four-domain web tasks constant, narrowing agent_authorization_testing by showing instruction-level least privilege still leaks about 37% of sensitive data when higher-sensitivity sources remain reachable whereas submission-time verification blocks over 96% without hurting task success.
- Language Model Security Modules: Turning LLM Interpretability into Runtime Defense ([arXiv:2608.25697](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25697)). operational technique. Rule 4: varies pluggable interpretability backends (probes, transcoder, SAE) and eager versus compiled vLLM execution while holding a shared fail-closed runtime mediation path and HarmBench/XSTest benchmarks constant, providing a directly usable policy-enforcement module that verifies required evidence checks executed before output release at over 96% native throughput.
- Securing and Auditing Long-Horizon LLM Agents: State-Transfer Auditing and Controls in the Agent Harness ([arXiv:2611.23346](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23346)). operational technique. Rule 4: varies harness instrumentation (hash-chained state-transfer audit middleware separating microsecond deterministic checks from semantic checks versus lifecycle and tool-only logs) while holding 240 multi-step fault-injection trajectories constant, providing a directly usable state-transfer auditing and runtime control harness that catches cross-transfer violations including compaction loss and stale KV cache.
- Weighing the Envelope: Component Weight and Arbitration in Envelope-Layer Defenses for a Single LLM Agent ([arXiv:2611.23844](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23844)). narrows. Varies tool-side forced-verification labels, poisoned memory dose, tool roles, and backends while holding single-agent MCP benign-trigger tasks fixed, narrowing Principle 4 and agent_authorization_testing under Rule 4 by showing envelope verification labels act as reversible soft weights eroded by memory wording rather than substitutes for hard permissions or sandboxing.
- Guarding the Kingdom of Skill-Based Agents in the AI Dynasty of Computation: Control-Flow Integrity for Agent Skills ([arXiv:2611.24185](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24185)). operational technique. Holds agent skill tasks constant while testing static allowlists against compile-time enforceable contracts and a deterministic runtime CFI enforcer (SkillCFI), providing a concrete containment mechanism that blocks ordering violations and regime migration before tool dispatch.
- Attacks and Defenses in Decentralized Multi-Agent Systems with Hop-by-Hop Task Decomposition and Delegation ([arXiv:2611.25056](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25056)). operational technique. Varies malicious-agent attacks and introduces a cryptographic verified-execution-trace protocol plus trace auditor across HiddenBench decentralized delegation tasks, detecting and attributing 98% of attacks at negligible latency overhead as a methodology-level verification defense under Rules 3 and 4.
- When History Overrides Intent: Relational Schema Lock-In in Human-LLM Interaction ([arXiv:2610.41367](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41367)). extends. Demonstrates across six models and a 360-participant human study that persistent conversational history causes assistants to silently override current user intent via artifact substitution and bypass human oversight (75% sent the substituted letter), extending the automation-bias and overreliance seed note (2026-06-30-automation-bias-overreliance.md) to LLM memory scope violations.
- The Vibe Paradox: What EEG Reveals About Your Brain When Vibe Coding ([arXiv:2611.02087](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02087)). extends. Rule 3: holds developer coding tasks constant while varying non-agentic chat versus agentic delegation (Cursor Agents) in a crossover EEG study, extending operator_attention by showing agent delegation lowers outcome engagement, raises cognitive load and trust, and shifts verification away from code reading.
- Evaluating Human-LLM Alignment on Visualization Preference ([arXiv:2611.06272](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06272)). extends. Pilot study comparing vision LLM-as-a-judge verdicts against human annotators on chart-preference pairs, extending Principle 5's caveat on nuanced quality judgments by showing frontier VLM judges collapse plural human preferences into near-deterministic verdicts.
- Supporting Researcher Control in AI-Assisted Social Media Analysis: A Mixed-Methods Study of AI4OSN ([arXiv:2611.22006](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22006)). extends. Extends the operator_attention seed note (2026-06-30-automation-bias-overreliance.md) with an N=18 mixed-methods study of human-controlled LLM annotation showing novice researchers exhibit greater alignment and higher agreement with AI outputs than experienced researchers.

## Ignore

- Working with AI: Measuring the Applicability of Generative AI to Occupations ([arXiv:2507.07935](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07935)). Analyzes 200k Copilot conversations to measure generative AI applicability and task delegation across occupations rather than verification or oversight of agent work, so ignore.
- Model-Adaptive Tool Necessity Reveals the Knowing-Doing Gap in LLM Tool Use ([arXiv:2605.14038](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14038)). Measures mismatches between model-adaptive tool necessity and actual tool calls on arithmetic and factual QA and probes late-layer hidden states to diagnose a knowing-doing gap in task-solving tool use, which is about agent capability rather than verifying agent work.
- MirrorCraft: Paired Evaluation under Hidden Rule Changes in Minecraft ([arXiv:2607.29218](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29218)). Introduces paired Minecraft worlds with hidden server-side rule changes to measure how agent architectures adapt to altered game mechanics; drop under Rules 4 and 5 as a game-simulation capability benchmark.
- From Target Selection to Condition Reuse: Auditing Residual Interventions in Tool-Use Models ([arXiv:2610.31686](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31686)). Activation-intervention audit in tool-use models; the finding is about model internals, not about a check on agent output.
- Before the Ticket: Grounding HPC User Support in Documentation and Resolved Tickets with an Agentic LLM Assistant ([arXiv:2610.31703](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31703)). Domain support assistant where the fine-tuned tool is the main result; the note that a selector or LLM verifier did not beat one tool is incidental and not characterized as a verifier-error study.
- Mining the Design Space of Multi-Agent Workflows ([arXiv:2610.31709](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31709)). Varies multi-agent workflow topologies and prompt templates via MCTS mining while holding 26 Enigmata tasks and their deterministic verifier constant; drop under Rules 1 and 3 as orchestration mining for task capability.
- Beyond On-Policy vs. Off-Policy: At What Level Should LLM Agent Distillation Follow the Student? ([arXiv:2610.31918](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31918)). Rule 1: holds interactive environments and teacher guidance constant while varying hierarchical on-policy versus off-policy agent distillation mixtures and schedules, which is learner-side so drop.
- Async-RBench: Benchmarking Asynchronous Task Execution in LLM Agents ([arXiv:2610.32429](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32429)). Benchmark of agents' asynchronous result handling that measures capability.
- Agentic Hazard Mapper: Emulating Expert Map-Making with AI Agents for Risk Communication ([arXiv:2610.32738](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32738)). Builds a multi-agent cartographic workflow for generating hazard maps and evaluates map utility with practitioners rather than testing a verification or oversight mechanism, so ignore.
- Closing the empirical loop: autonomous AI agents conduct end-to-end research with human participants ([arXiv:2508.13421](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13421)). Demonstrates an autonomous AI agent conducting end-to-end psychology experiments with human participants without evaluating verification, oversight, or authorization boundaries, so ignore.
- Hierarchical Alignment: Block-Level LoRA-DPO for Preference Tuning in Large Language Models ([arXiv:2510.12044](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.12044)). Varies transformer-block placement of a fixed LoRA budget during DPO training while holding preference data and evaluation judges constant, making this a learner-side training study under Rule 1, ignore.
- Agentic AI Empowered Intent-Based Networking for 6G ([arXiv:2601.06640](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.06640)). Rule 3: varies a hierarchical multi-agent ReAct orchestrator for 6G intent-based network slice synthesis while holding domain benchmarks constant, targeting telecommunications task capability rather than verification.
- Navigating Ideation Space: Decomposed Conceptual Representations for Positioning Scientific Ideas ([arXiv:2601.08901](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.08901)). Retrieval and novelty-assessment representation for scientific ideas, a task system not a check on agent work.
- Multi-Agentic AI for Fairness-Aware and Accelerated Multi-modal Large Model Inference in Real-world Mobile Edge Networks ([arXiv:2602.07215](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07215)). Varies a multi-agent mobile-edge prompt-routing and deployment controller while holding multimodal models fixed to optimize inference latency and fairness rather than verification allocation, so ignore.
- Automatic Configuration of LLM Post-Training Pipelines ([arXiv:2603.18773](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.18773)). Holds biomedical post-training tasks constant while varying budget-aware hyperparameter optimization for SFT and RL pipelines, which is training-compute configuration rather than verification allocation under Rule 3.
- AutoB2G: Agentic Simulation and Reinforcement Learning for Spatio-Temporal Grid-Interactive Building Control ([arXiv:2603.26005](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.26005)). Presents an energy-grid co-simulation environment and LLM orchestration pipeline for composing building-control simulations without varying or evaluating verification mechanisms, so ignore.
- LinearARD: Linear-Memory Attention Distillation for RoPE Restoration ([arXiv:2604.00004](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00004)). Proposes a linear-memory attention distillation method (LinearARD) for RoPE context-window extension rather than verification of agent work, so ignore.
- Repetition over Diversity: High-Signal Data Filtering for Sample-Efficient German Language Modeling ([arXiv:2604.28075](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.28075)). Pretraining data-filtering study comparing multi-epoch repetition against corpus diversity for German language modeling with no bearing on agent verification.
- Terminus-4B: Can a Smaller Model Replace Frontier LLMs at Agentic Execution Tasks? ([arXiv:2605.03195](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03195)). Holds rubric-based LLM-judge training rewards and coding benchmarks constant while post-training a 4B subagent to cut main-agent token use, which is learner-side training and compute routing under Rules 1 and 3.
- Where and What to Reward? Understanding Generalization Between Verifiable and Open-Ended Domains in Reinforcement Learning ([arXiv:2605.15464](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15464)). Rule 1: holds the RL optimizer and prompt count constant while varying training prompt domains and rule-based vs learned rewards to study cross-domain policy generalization without measuring reward checking accuracy, which is learner-side so drop.
- TwinRouterBench: Fast Static and Live Dynamic Evaluation for Realistic Agentic LLM Routing ([arXiv:2605.18859](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18859)). Rule 3: introduces TwinRouterBench holding execution-verified step prefixes and SWE-bench tasks constant to evaluate generator-model routers that cut API spend while preserving task resolution, which is generator cost-quality routing rather than verifier routing, so drop.
- Learning Faithful Reasoning Processes with Spatio-Temporal Latent Dynamics for Large Language Models ([arXiv:2605.26893](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26893)). Varies latent-uncertainty and entropy-dynamics process regularization inside GRPO training while holding outcome verification fixed and reporting only trained policy accuracy and efficiency, which is learner-side under Rule 1, ignore.
- The LLENS Platform for On-Premises Large Language Model Agents in a Japanese University Hospital: Development and Evaluation Study ([arXiv:2610.32151](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32151)). Clinical deployment case study varying retrieval versus interactive document-tool access while holding hospital QA tasks fixed to measure clinical task accuracy and throughput rather than testing containment or verification mechanisms under Rules 4 and 5.
- RADAgent: Automating RADseq Pipelines with Private Open-Source LLMs ([arXiv:2610.33009](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33009)). Domain workflow automation agent with modular verification and no finding about the check.
- Scaling Qualitative Analysis with Large Language Models: Development and Validation of the Smart Coder Framework for Human-LLM Collaborative Coding ([arXiv:2610.33205](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33205)). Prompting framework for LLM qualitative coding; the note that self-consistency flags unstable codes for human review is minor and unquantified.
- BenchBench-Cloning: Evaluating Molecular Cloning Design and Protocol Development ([arXiv:2610.33355](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33355)). Introduces BenchBench-Cloning to measure frontier LLM capability on 65 molecular cloning protocol and construct-design tasks with fixed simulator and judge checks, which is a domain capability benchmark rather than a verification study.
- A Comprehensive Framework for All Stages of Large-Model Training: From Data and Tokenization, through Pretraining, to Post-Training Alignment ([arXiv:2610.33451](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33451)). Surveys end-to-end LLM and MLLM training pipelines from tokenization through post-training alignment without varying or evaluating verification checks under rule 1, so ignore.
- LLMs in Cloud: A Discussion on Architecture, Orchestration, and Inference ([arXiv:2610.33475](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33475)). Reviews cloud datacenter LLM serving, hardware virtualization, and cluster orchestration infrastructure with no bearing on verifying agent work or oversight design, so ignore.
- AlphaIdeaBench: Benchmarking Automated Research Idea Generation under Feasibility Constraints with Peer Review Alignment ([arXiv:2610.33644](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33644)). Research-ideation benchmark; the judge validation against reviewer scores supports the benchmark and the findings are about harness and backbone capability.
- WebViSE: Scaling Visual Design Intelligence from the Open Web for LLM Website Generation ([arXiv:2610.33929](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33929)). Extracts reusable visual design skills into a DAG from open websites to improve LLM website generation aesthetics, which is a domain generation capability method outside agent-work verification.
- Repairing Broken Access Control in Web Applications with LLMs: An Empirical Benchmark Study ([arXiv:2610.34290](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34290)). Varies base LLMs, agents, and repair scaffolds while holding a sandboxed web-application broken-access-control benchmark (BAC-Bench) fixed to measure code-patching capability rather than agent authorization or verification checks, so ignore.
- Large Language Models: A Plain-Language Overview for Engineers ([arXiv:2610.34585](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34585)). Provides a plain-language introductory overview of LLM fundamentals for engineers with no research findings or verification methods beyond briefly flagging contamination and LLM judges, so ignore.
- Audio-R1: Audio Reasoning through Reinforcement Learning on Self-Supervised Tasks ([arXiv:2610.34609](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34609)). Varies RL post-training of audio-language models using fixed self-supervised verifiable rewards and reports only downstream audio benchmark gains; drop under Rule 1 as learner-side RL training.
- AIPC: Evidence-Guided Agent Workflows for PyTorch Project Deployment ([arXiv:2610.34890](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34890)). Varies skill-guided deployment workflows against a control while holding PyTorch-to-NPU toolchains and acceptance criteria fixed to improve hardware porting capability rather than studying verification under Rules 1 and 3.
- Context Is Not Free: Carbon-Aware LLM Routing for Multi-Turn LLM Web Services across Geo-Distributed Data Centers ([arXiv:2610.35230](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35230)). Rule 3: varies geo-distributed model, GPU, and region routing accounting for KV-cache recomputation while holding multi-turn web workloads constant, optimizing serving carbon and latency rather than verification or oversight routing.
- Before Blaming the LLM, Diagnose the Workflow: A Role-Level Difficulty Profile for Translating Natural Language into Ontology-Grounded Statements ([arXiv:2610.35267](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35267)). Qualitative diagnostic profile for NL-to-DSL workflows with no empirical finding.
- EvoRouter: Cost-Efficient Multi-Agent Routing via Insight-Guided On-Policy Distillation ([arXiv:2610.35690](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35690)). Varies insight-guided on-policy distillation and cost-aware RL for multi-agent configuration routing (EvoRouter) while holding task benchmarks fixed to trade inference compute against accuracy; this is generator-side routing and orchestration under rule 3, so ignore.
- AVIATOR: Towards AI-Agentic Vulnerability Injection Workflow for High-Fidelity, Large-Scale Code Security Dataset ([arXiv:2508.20866](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.20866)). Agentic data-generation workflow varying multi-agent vulnerability injection to augment code-security training datasets rather than verifying agent work or testing containment boundaries under Rule 4.
- Efficient Token-Level Policy Optimization for Masked Diffusion Models via Velocity Alignment ([arXiv:2603.01563](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01563)). Rule 1: holds verifiable math and coding rewards constant while varying the policy optimization algorithm (LFPO velocity alignment) for diffusion LLMs, which is learner-side so drop.
- Dr. Post-Training: A Data Regularization Perspective on LLM Post-Training ([arXiv:2605.07063](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07063)). Holds SFT, RLHF, and RLVR rewards fixed while varying data-regularized update projections to improve post-training performance, which is learner-side under Rule 1.
- PIVOT: Bridging Planning and Execution in LLM Agents via Trajectory Refinement ([arXiv:2605.11225](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11225)). Rule 1: varies an iterative trajectory-refinement loop using textual gradients while holding task environments and constraints constant, targeting agent planning capability and token efficiency rather than evaluating verification.
- Does The Way You Plan Matter? An Empirical Study of Planning Representations for LLM Web Agents ([arXiv:2605.29927](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29927)). Varies four natural-language planning representations across web agents while holding WebArena tasks fixed to measure task-completion capability and consistency rather than verification of agent work under Rule 3.
- UnityBench-Mini3D: Evaluating LLM Agents for 3D Game Development in Unity ([arXiv:2610.35980](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35980)). Unity game-development benchmark with fixed milestone tests; it measures agent capability and does not study the tests as a check.
- Log-To-Leak: Prompt Injection Attacks on Tool-Using LLM Agents via the Model Context Protocol ([arXiv:2610.36108](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36108)). Rule 4: the contribution is a prompt-injection attack on tool-using agents, which stays out regardless of its defense observations.
- BUSTA: Teaching Small Language Models to Reason About Binary Execution ([arXiv:2610.36288](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36288)). Rule 1: varies GRPO fine-tuning of small models on inverse x86-64 basic-block execution while holding the execution verifier constant as a fixed training environment, targeting reverse-engineering capability rather than verification.
- Detecting and Mitigating Query-Based System Prompt Leakage Attacks ([arXiv:2610.36295](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36295)). Prompt-leakage attack detector for LLM services, a security classifier rather than a check on agent work.
- kw-bench: Breaking Frontier Harnesses on Real-World Tasks ([arXiv:2610.36405](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36405)). Introduces a deterministic investigative-accounting benchmark and varies agent harnesses and models while holding grading fixed to measure knowledge-work capability, so ignore under Rule 5.
- Verifiable Efficiency Limits of Innate LLM Planning in Sliding Puzzles ([arXiv:2610.36415](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36415)). Planning-efficiency benchmark on sliding puzzles; verifiable scoring is the fixed instrument and the finding is about unaided planning capability.
- RoboAuto: Autonomous Robot Program Search through Multi-Level Physical Understanding ([arXiv:2610.36533](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36533)). Robot program search framework, a capability result.
- The Vocabulary Is Already Here: Adapting Pretrained Interpretable Features for Generative Recommendation ([arXiv:2610.36763](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36763)). Adapts pretrained sparse autoencoder features into semantic IDs for generative recommendation, which is domain representation learning unrelated to verifying agent work.
- When All Rollouts Fail: Policy-Compatible Joint Decoding for Hard-Problem Reasoning ([arXiv:2610.36849](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36849)). Holds verifiable reasoning rewards constant while varying expert-guided joint decoding and mixed-behavior RL optimization (PICO) for hard problems, which is learner-side training under Rule 1.
- Guided-RLVR: Guided Exploration in Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards ([arXiv:2610.37041](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37041)). Holds verifiable math and code rewards constant while varying a steering-token controller for RLVR exploration, which is learner-side training under Rule 1.
- Exploring the Limits of the Delta Learning Hypothesis: Single-Pair Generator Biases in Direct Preference Optimization ([arXiv:2610.37058](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37058)). Preference-data construction for DPO, a learner-side training finding.
- Bayes-Adaptive MDPs for Rollout Allocation to Enhance GRPO Training ([arXiv:2610.37429](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37429)). Rule 1: varies difficulty-aware rollout budget allocation via a Bayes-adaptive MDP during GRPO training while holding reasoning datasets, verifiable rewards, and total rollout budget constant to improve trained policy performance, which is learner-side RL training, so drop.
- PerceptRL: Making Visual Perception Rewardable in MLLMs from Answers to Interactions ([arXiv:2610.37471](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37471)). Varies multi-agent caption-error QA rewards, coordinate injection, and a grounding-aware process reward inside MLLM RL post-training while reporting only trained-policy benchmark accuracy rather than reward accuracy as a check, which is learner-side under Rule 1, ignore.
- Follow the Gradient: Tracing Safety Degradation Back to Training Data ([arXiv:2610.37481](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37481)). Develops a scalable gradient-attribution filter (SimpleLoRIF) to remove safety-degrading examples from fine-tuning datasets, which is training-data curation unrelated to verifying agent work.
- Rethinking Bayesian Optimization for Co-Optimizing LLM Training Configurations ([arXiv:2602.08351](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08351)). Varies Bayesian optimization budget allocation between performance-predictor fitting and configuration search while holding LLM fine-tuning tasks fixed to optimize training efficiency rather than verification allocation, so ignore.
- Learning to Model Optimization Problems from Solver Outcomes ([arXiv:2604.00442](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.00442)). Varies the training method (outcome-based RL versus process-supervised SFT) while holding the mathematical programming solver and sandbox fixed as the reward environment and reporting only trained model optimization-modeling performance, so ignore.
- An Analysis of Artificial Intelligence Adoption in NIH-Funded Research ([arXiv:2604.07424](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07424)). Uses an LLM classification pipeline to analyze AI adoption and health-disparities funding across 58,746 NIH grants for research policy rather than studying verification of agent work, so ignore.
- Adaptive Query Routing: A Tier-Based Framework for Hybrid Retrieval Across Financial, Legal, and Medical Documents ([arXiv:2604.14222](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.14222)). - AEM: Adaptive Entropy Modulation for Multi-Turn Agentic Reinforcement Learning ([arXiv:2605.00425](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00425)). Rule 1: holds sparse outcome-only environment rewards constant while varying response-level entropy advantage rescaling (AEM) for multi-turn agentic RL credit assignment, which is learner-side so drop.
- ASTOR: Multi-Task Code Reinforcement Learning via Utility-Driven Coordination ([arXiv:2605.06111](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06111)). Varies multi-task RLVR data scheduling and per-task KL regularization (ASTOR) while holding verifiable code rewards fixed and reporting only trained policy coding performance, so ignore.
- DUET: Optimize Token-Budget Allocation for Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards ([arXiv:2605.08441](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08441)). Rule 1: varies RLVR rollout allocation and early-abort token budgeting over GRPO while holding verifiable rewards constant, optimizing policy training speed and accuracy rather than verification design.
- CHI-Bench: Can AI Agents Automate End-to-End, Long-Horizon, Policy-Rich Healthcare Workflows? ([arXiv:2605.16679](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16679)). Rule 4: holds a simulated 20-app policy-rich healthcare workflow environment constant to measure 30 agent configurations' end-to-end task completion, making it a domain capability benchmark rather than a verification or containment study.
- Executable Schema Contracts: From Automatic Ingestion to Multi-Source Retrieval ([arXiv:2606.05415](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05415)). Rule 3: varies automatic schema-contract discovery and schema-conditioned multi-tool retrieval routing while holding the LLM, data, and QA evaluation harness constant to improve QA accuracy, which is a retrieval capability system rather than verification of agent work, so drop.
- INFRAMIND: Infrastructure-Aware Multi-Agent Orchestration ([arXiv:2606.11440](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11440)). - LLM-INSTRUCT at UZH Shared Task 2026: Constraint-Aware Retrieval and Selective Debate for Paragraph-Level Argument Mining ([arXiv:2607.20430](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20430)). - From Profiles to Steering Vectors: Global Sparse Priors and Local Semantic Calibration for Personalized Text Generation ([arXiv:2607.21620](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21620)). Varies a sparse-autoencoder activation steering method for personalized text generation while holding LLM-as-judge and ROUGE metrics constant as evaluation tools, so the finding is about generation capability rather than verification.

- An Introduction to Large Language Models for Scientists and Professionals ([arXiv:2610.37684](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37684)). Introductory lecture textbook covering general LLM concepts and responsible use for scientists and professionals with no new verification finding or method.
- From Geometry to Behavior: How Instruction Exposure Unlocks Latent Rhetorical Directions in LLMs ([arXiv:2610.37685](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37685)). Mechanistic interpretability study of activation-steering analogy vectors across base and instruction-tuned models with no bearing on checking agent work, so drop.

- Value Probes: Learned Value Functions for Beam-Search Red Teaming ([arXiv:2610.38526](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38526)). Varies automated red-teaming search via beam search guided by linear activation Value Probes while holding the LLM judge and target safety behaviors constant; drop under Rule 2 as search-efficiency optimization over a fixed judge.
- Latent Decision Flow Rewards for Clinical Guideline Reasoning ([arXiv:2610.38709](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38709)). Under Rule 1, holds clinical guideline QA benchmarks constant and jointly trains a latent decision-flow reward controller with the reasoning policy, reporting only trained policy accuracy and adherence rather than verifier accuracy, so ignore.
- Reasoning or Pattern Exploitation? Mechanistic Insights from RL-Trained Language Models ([arXiv:2610.39182](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39182)). Uses sparse autoencoders to probe internal feature reorganization of a GRPO-trained policy in Tic-Tac-Toe while holding the game verifier fixed, focusing on learner representations rather than verification mechanisms under Rule 1, ignore.

- Online Dense Reinforcement Learning with Planning-Grounding Agent for UI Navigation on Live IOS Apps ([arXiv:2610.39264](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39264)). Holds an LLM-as-a-Judge reward and live iOS app environment constant while varying online PPO/GiGPPO training and planning-grounding agent architecture, making it learner-side capability training under Rule 1.

Editorial disposition after review; intake label narrows. The paper reports the table VQA accuracy of an inference-time generator, critic and refiner loop. It does not measure the accuracy, coverage or failure modes of the internal check or compare it with independent verification, so the finding concerns the learner's capability.

## Batch accounting

Total: 1016 = 123 already-handled + 562 ignore + 24 full-record + 307 deferred.
Intake labels (893): ignore 561, operational technique 156, narrows 93, extends 80, challenges 3. One intake narrows entry (arXiv:2610.32423) is listed under Ignore by editorial disposition, which is why the Ignore section holds 562 entries.
Full-record decisions: 16 promote, 8 keep-in-triage, 0 keep-in-scout, 0 ignore.
Basis: 733 full-abstract-agreed, 160 full-abstract-tiebreak, 123 already-handled.
